* Consequences: `docs/PROJECT.md`, `docs/ACCEPTANCE.md` and `README.md` replace the public home page with the login page as entry point. The privacy notice is linked on all pages accessible without login (D-022).

## D-026: Minimum Versions and Package Selection

* Date: 2026-10-11
* Status: Decided (project owner, 2026-10-11: minimum versions, `segno` and `msal`, and the custom rate-limit component confirmed; see "Decisions of the project owner" below). The database rows (MySQL) are superseded by MariaDB 10.11 as minimum and 12.3 LTS as used version (update at the end of this decision, D-030).
* Context: README "Current Status", next step 2: determine the minimum versions of Python, Django, MySQL and the intended packages from official sources (D-015) before the Django project skeleton is created.
* Proposal:

  | Component | Proposed minimum | Basis |
  | --------- | ---------------- | ----- |
  | Python | 3.11 | 3.10 reached end of life on 2026-10-01; 3.11 receives security fixes until 2027-10 (scheduled). Django 6.x requires Python ≥ 3.12, so the minimum combination is Python 3.11 with Django 5.2. |
  | Django | 5.2 LTS, at least the latest patch 5.2.18 | Extended support ("Security fixes and data loss bugs") until April 2028. Django 5.2 supports Python 3.10 to 3.14 (3.14 added in 5.2.8). Django's download page: the answer to whether to upgrade to the latest patch release "will always be 'yes.'" |
  | MySQL | 8.4 LTS | "As of April 2026, with version 8.0.46, MySQL 8.0 reaches End of Life (EoL)." An LTS series "follows the Oracle Lifetime Support Policy, which includes 5 years of premier support and 3 years of extended support"; 8.4.0 was released on 2024-04-30 (end dates derived from this, not stated as dates by Oracle). Latest patch: 8.4.12 (2026-08-18). Django 5.2 supports MySQL 8.0.11 and higher. |
  | `mysqlclient` | 2.3.0 | Django: "the recommended choice"; Django requires ≥ 1.4.3. Release 2026-09-14, Python ≥ 3.10, Windows wheels for CPython 3.10 to 3.15. |
  | `django-axes` | 8.3.1 | Login abuse protection (D-017). Release 2026-02-11, Python ≥ 3.10, declares Django 4.2, 5.2, 6.0 and Python 3.10 to 3.14. |
  | `django-ipware` | 8.0.0 | Trusted-proxy-aware client IP resolution for login abuse protection (D-017); supports Python 3.10+ and Django 5.2+. Its required `python-ipware` dependency is installed transitively. |
  | `django-otp` | 1.7.4 | TOTP and static recovery tokens (D-004). Release 2026-10-08, Python ≥ 3.8; built-in exponential throttling of failed verifications (not used in the project's own device models, D-040). Offers the extras `qrcode` and `segno`. |
  | `cryptography` | 50.0.2 | HKDF and Fernet for the Microsoft Graph certificate private key (D-006), second factor secrets (D-040) and keyed hashes (D-023). Python ≥ 3.9 (except 3.9.0, 3.9.1). |
  | `segno` (new) | 1.6.6 | QR code for TOTP enrolment in the application views; pure Python, BSD licence, no dependencies on Python ≥ 3.10; one of the two extras offered by `django-otp`. |
  | `msal` (new) | 1.39.0 | Token acquisition for Microsoft Graph with the client credentials flow; Microsoft's own library, MIT licence, Python ≥ 3.9. Depends on `requests`, `PyJWT[crypto]` and `cryptography<51`. |
  | Tabler | 1.6.1 | Latest release (28 September); served by the instance itself (D-024). |

  * Versions used (D-015, newest possible version): development uses Django 6.1 (latest 6.1.2; supports Python 3.12 to 3.14) with Python 3.14 (latest 3.14.8, 2026-09-30; Python 3.15.0 was released on 2026-10-09 but is not yet supported by any Django release) and MySQL 9.7 LTS (D-027; superseded on 2026-10-11 by MariaDB 12.3 LTS, D-030), and the newest releases of all packages. The code must also run with the minimum versions; continuous integration tests both ends. `django-axes` 8.3.1 declares Django only up to 6.0; its compatibility with Django 6.1 is proven by the tests, otherwise development falls back to Django 6.0.
  * Minimum patch levels are the current releases at the time of the decision, because older patch releases of these components may contain fixed vulnerabilities; whether an older patch release would be free of known vulnerabilities was not checked for each package.
* Not recommended: `django-ratelimit` for the per-IP and per-address limits of registration, password reset and email change (D-017). Its latest release 4.1.0 dates from 2023-07-24 and declares Python only up to 3.11. Alternative: a small, tested component on Django's cache framework (`django.core.cache`) with the keyed hash from D-023. This is a custom development for a security function and therefore requires the project owner's decision.
* Open, requires the project owner:
  * confirmation of the minimum versions and of the new packages `segno` and `msal`;
  * per-IP and per-address limits for registration, password reset and email change (login account/IP limits were decided separately in D-017 on 2026-10-12);
  * a local MySQL 8.4 server for development and tests (none is installed on the development machine; Docker is not installed); tests depend on MySQL behaviour (unique index with several `NULL` values, D-016) and cannot be replaced by SQLite;
  * how the minimum versions are proven by tests (D-015): only Python 3.13 (and the end-of-life 3.8) is installed locally; a test matrix with Python 3.11 needs a further local Python installation or continuous integration, and continuous integration on an external service publishes project content and requires consent;
  * `msal` caps `cryptography` below 51; a future security release of `cryptography` 51 could only be installed once `msal` allows it. Alternative: request the token with `requests` directly against the documented Microsoft identity platform endpoint (custom code, not recommended).
* Security implications: all proposed versions receive security updates from their vendors as of 2026-10-11. Python 3.11 reaches end of life in October 2027 and Django 5.2 in April 2028; the minimum versions are raised then (D-015). Superseded on 2026-10-11 by the update below: the minimums are now Python 3.12 (security fixes until 2028-10, scheduled) and Django 6.0 (security fixes until April 2027). `msal` adds `requests` and `PyJWT` as transitive dependencies.
* Sources (all checked on 2026-10-11): https://devguide.python.org/versions/, https://www.djangoproject.com/download/, https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/5.2/faq/install/, https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/5.2/ref/databases/, https://dev.mysql.com/doc/relnotes/mysql/8.0/en/, https://dev.mysql.com/doc/relnotes/mysql/8.4/en/, https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/mysql-releases.html, https://dev.mysql.com/doc/relnotes/mysql/8.4/en/news-8-4-0.html ("Changes in MySQL 8.4.0 (2024-04-30)"), https://pypi.org/project/mysqlclient/, https://pypi.org/pypi/django-axes/json, https://pypi.org/project/django-otp/, https://django-otp-official.readthedocs.io/en/stable/overview.html, https://pypi.org/pypi/cryptography/json, https://pypi.org/pypi/segno/json, https://pypi.org/pypi/msal/json, https://pypi.org/pypi/django-ratelimit/json, https://github.com/tabler/tabler/releases
* Decisions of the project owner (2026-10-11):
  * The minimum versions and the packages `segno` and `msal` are confirmed.
  * The per-IP and per-address limits of registration, password reset and email change are implemented as a small, tested component on Django's cache framework instead of `django-ratelimit`. Because the counters must be shared between all worker processes, a shared cache backend is required (Django's local-memory cache is per process); the backend is chosen at implementation and recorded.
  * The local MySQL server is installed with Chocolatey (D-027).
  * The minimum versions are proven by continuous integration; the project is made public for this purpose (D-027).
* Consequences: the dependency file is created and the Django project skeleton is set up.
* Update (project owner, 2026-10-11): the minimum versions of Django and Python are raised:

  | Component | New minimum | Basis |
  | --------- | ----------- | ----- |
  | Django | 6.0, at least the latest patch 6.0.9 | The project uses Django's built-in Content Security Policy support (D-028). Django 6.0 release notes: "Built-in support for the Content Security Policy (CSP) standard is now available ... headers are added via the ContentSecurityPolicyMiddleware, nonces are supported through the csp() context processor, and policies are configured using the SECURE_CSP and SECURE_CSP_REPORT_ONLY settings". 6.0 is the oldest supported release line with this function (D-015). Django's download page (checked on 2026-10-11): 6.0, latest release 6.0.9, end of extended support April 2027; 6.1, latest 6.1.2, December 2027; 5.2 LTS, latest 5.2.18, April 2028. The minimum must be raised when 6.0 support ends in April 2027. |
  | Python | 3.12 | "Django 6.0 supports Python 3.12, 3.13, and 3.14" (release notes); `Requires-Python: >=3.12` in the metadata of Django 6.0.9 and 6.1.2. Python 3.12 receives security fixes until 2028-10 (scheduled; https://devguide.python.org/versions/, checked on 2026-10-11). Python 3.11 is no longer possible. |

  * The other packages keep their minimum versions. Their metadata (installed versions, 2026-10-11) allows Python 3.12: `mysqlclient` (>= 3.10, classifiers 3.10 to 3.14), `django-axes` (>= 3.10; declares Django 4.2, 5.2 and 6.0), `django-otp` (>= 3.8), `cryptography` (>= 3.9 except 3.9.0 and 3.9.1), `segno` (>= 3.5), `msal` (>= 3.9); all transitive dependencies require 3.10 or lower.
  * Django 6.0.9 supports MySQL 8.0.11 and later, Django 6.1.2 MySQL 8.4 and later (`minimum_database_version` in `django/db/backends/mysql/features.py` of both installed versions); the minimum MySQL 8.4 (above) is unchanged. (Superseded by the database update below.)
  * `pyproject.toml`: `requires-python = ">=3.12"`, `Django>=6.0.9,<6.2`; `requirements/minimum-constraints.txt`: `Django==6.0.9`; the CI minimum job uses Python 3.12 and checks the Django 6.0 release line.
  * Django 6.0 does not know the `MAILERS` setting (not in its `global_settings.py`; Django's test utilities override `EMAIL_BACKEND` in 6.0 and every `MAILERS` alias in 6.1), so the settings keep both branches (D-028). No other compatibility code for Django 5.2 existed.
  * Sources: https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.0/releases/6.0/, https://www.djangoproject.com/download/, https://devguide.python.org/versions/, https://pypi.org/project/Django/ (6.0.9 is the latest 6.0 release; `pip index versions Django`, 2026-10-11), installed sources of Django 6.0.9 and 6.1.2
* Update (project owner, 2026-10-11): the database changes from MySQL to MariaDB (D-002, D-030):

  | Component | Minimum | Used (newest) | Basis |
  | --------- | ------- | ------------- | ----- |
  | MariaDB | 10.11 | 12.3 LTS (latest patch 12.3.3) | 10.11 is the oldest long-term support release that still receives community support (end 16 February 2028) and the minimum of Django 6.1 ("Django supports MariaDB 10.11 and higher"); Django 6.0 supports 10.6 and higher, but 10.6 reached the end of community support on 6 July 2026. 12.3 is the newest long-term support release (community support until 12 June 2029). Details and sources in D-030. |

  * `mysqlclient` stays the database driver; Django recommends it for MariaDB as well ("To use MariaDB, use the MySQL backend").

## D-027: Local Database, Public Repository and Continuous Integration

* Date: 2026-10-11
* Status: Decided; the local database part is superseded by D-030 (project owner, 2026-10-11: MariaDB instead of MySQL)
* Note (2026-10-11, D-030): the local database is now MariaDB 12.3 LTS, installed with the Chocolatey package `mariadb.install` below `%ProgramFiles%`, and continuous integration tests MariaDB 10.11 and 12.3. Everything below that concerns MySQL (MySQL 9.7 and 8.4, the Chocolatey package `mysql`, the layout `C:\LEG-MySQL`, X Plugin, `mysqld-auto.cnf`, `.mylogin.cnf`, MySQL's reserved accounts) is kept as history of the earlier setup. Still valid: configuration in user environment variables, the public repository, the hardening of continuous integration, and the manual removal of the earlier MySQL installation (`C:\tools\mysql`, `C:\ProgramData\MySQL\data`), which the setup script still prints.
* Context: Tests depend on MySQL behaviour (D-016), and the minimum versions must be proven by tests (D-015, D-026). The development machine had neither MySQL nor Docker, and only Python 3.13.
* Decision:
  * Local development and tests use MySQL installed with Chocolatey (project owner, 2026-10-11). The local server runs MySQL 9.7 LTS, not the minimum version 8.4: the minimum version (D-026) states what the software must support, not what an instance runs. 9.7 is supported longer, and the Chocolatey package is closer to Oracle's latest patch for 9.7 (9.7.2 vs. 9.7.3) than for 8.4 (8.4.6 vs. 8.4.12). The project owner asked on 2026-10-11 why 9.7 LTS was not used.
  * The local setup is done by `scripts/local/setup-dev-windows.ps1`, which the developer runs once as administrator (installation of a Windows service requires elevation). The settings read their configuration from environment variables, the method shown in Django's deployment checklist (`SECRET_KEY = os.environ["SECRET_KEY"]`). Locally, the script stores the configuration as user environment variables without printing generated values; how `.env` is loaded into the environment on servers is decided with the hosting (D-008). AI tools therefore never need to read a secret file.
  * Continuous integration tests both the minimum version (MySQL 8.4) and the latest LTS (MySQL 9.7). Whether Django 6.0 and 6.1 work correctly with MySQL 9.7 is not stated by Django (minimum supported versions in the installed sources: MySQL 8.0.11 for Django 6.0.9, MySQL 8.4 for Django 6.1.2, `minimum_database_version` in `django/db/backends/mysql/features.py`) and is proven by these tests (updated on 2026-10-11 after the minimum was raised to Django 6.0, D-026).
  * The repository is published on GitHub and continuous integration runs there with GitHub Actions; the project owner consented to making the project public (2026-10-11).
* Verified facts:
  * The Chocolatey package `mysql` (community package, not maintained by Oracle) offers 8.4.6 as its latest 8.4 version (Oracle: 8.4.12, 2026-08-18) and 9.7.2 as its latest 9.7 version (Oracle: 9.7.3, 2026-08-18).
  * MySQL 9.7 is an LTS series: Oracle's reference manual names the repository `mysql-9.7-lts-community` for 9.7.x releases, and the 9.7.0 release notes (2026-04-21) refer to "consecutive LTS versions higher than 9.7.0". An LTS series receives "5 years of premier support and 3 years of extended support"; no end date is stated for 9.7. 9.7 is the last release line before calendar versioning (YY.M). It downloads the official zip archive from `cdn.mysql.com`, checks a SHA-256 checksum, installs a Windows service and initialises the data directory with `--initialize-insecure`, i.e. the `root` account has no password. The package sets no bind address.
  * Commits use the GitHub no-reply address as author email; no private email address is published with the history.
* Security implications:
  * Local MySQL (since the update below, the settings are written before the first start instead of afterwards): without hardening, `root` has no password and the server listens on all interfaces, both with the classic protocol (`bind_address` default `*`) and with X Plugin, which is enabled by default and listens on port 33060 on all interfaces (`mysqlx_bind_address` default `*`). The setup script therefore binds the server to `127.0.0.1`, disables X Plugin (`mysqlx=OFF`; Django does not use the X Protocol), verifies the listening sockets after the restart, sets a `root` password, verifies a login with it, and creates an application account with only the privileges Django needs (`SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, CREATE, DROP, ALTER, INDEX, REFERENCES`; superseded: `REFERENCES` is no longer granted, see D-049 decision 6) on the project database and its test database (`docs/INSTALLATION.md`, "Database privileges"). The root password is validated before anything is installed; if a later step fails, the script stops and disables the MySQL service and prints recovery steps (security review of 2026-10-11, M-2 to M-4, L-3, L-4). 9.7.2 lacks the fixes of 9.7.3; it is used only for local development, bound to the loopback interface, and is upgraded once the Chocolatey package offers a newer patch.
  * Residual risk of user environment variables: they are inherited by every process of the Windows user, including AI tools and their subprocesses, which can therefore read `DB_PASSWORD` and `DJANGO_SECRET_KEY` of the local environment. These are local development credentials only (no stage or production secrets on development machines, D-020). The environment is never printed (`AGENTS.md`, section 8).
  * Public repository: all documents, including the security policy, become public. They contain no secrets (checked with a pattern search before publishing). Security through obscurity is not relied upon. GitHub's secret scanning with push protection and Dependabot alerts are enabled.
  * Continuous integration: workflow permissions are read-only, no repository secrets are used, and third-party actions are pinned to full commit SHAs (GitHub documentation "Security hardening for GitHub Actions").
* Update (project owner, 2026-10-11, after the first run of the setup script failed right after `choco install mysql`):
  * Cause: the package registers the service with `mysqld --install MySQL`, so its command line is `C:\tools\mysql\current\bin\mysqld MySQL` (no quotes, no `.exe`, no `--defaults-file`; verified on the development machine with `Win32_Service.PathName`). The script took `C:\tools\mysql\current\bin\mysqld` as the program path, which does not exist as a file, and stopped. The option file is `C:\tools\mysql\current\my.ini` (`BASEDIR\my.ini`), so the existing fallback to the base directory was correct.
  * Fix: the program path is resolved from the service command line by trying the white-space separated prefixes from the shortest to the longest (the order Microsoft documents for `CreateProcess`) and, for a path without extension, the path itself and then the path with `.exe`. `datadir` values in `my.ini` are unescaped (`\\` stands for `\`), because the package writes `datadir=C:\\ProgramData\\MySQL\\data`.
  * New switch `-RemoveFailedInstallation`: removes a failed installation (stop the service, `choco uninstall mysql`, `sc.exe delete MySQL`, delete leftover `my.ini` files) and then runs the normal setup. The data directory is deleted only if it is the package default `C:\ProgramData\MySQL\data`, looks like a MySQL data directory (or is empty), and the user types `DELETE`; any other `datadir` is reported, never deleted. Without the switch, the script stops before changing anything if a service, the package, the data directory or a leftover `my.ini` exists, and shows the one-line command to run.
  * Security review of 2026-10-11 (M-1, M-2, L-1): the removal works only on an installation of the Chocolatey package: the service program must lie under `<tools>\mysql` (Chocolatey's tools directory, normalised full paths compared case-insensitively) and `--defaults-file` must be absent or point to `<tools>\mysql\current\my.ini`; only `<tools>\mysql\current\my.ini` and `my.ini.before-leg-setup` are deleted. Otherwise the script stops without changes and refers to the manual steps. If `C:\ProgramData\MySQL`, the data directory, `<tools>\mysql` or `<tools>\mysql\current` is a reparse point (symbolic link, junction, mount point), the script stops. Every removal requires typing `DELETE` after a list of all items. The removal first stops the service and sets it to `Disabled`. The data directory is re-checked (reparse points, MySQL data directory) immediately before it is deleted with `[IO.Directory]::Delete(path, true)`, which "does not recurse through the reparse point" (Microsoft Learn, "Directory.Delete Method"). Without the switch, the script warns if an existing `MySQL` service is running.
  * Security re-check of 2026-10-11 (N-1, N-2): the removal re-checks the ownership of the service at its start and the reparse points of `<tools>\mysql`, `<tools>\mysql\current` and the option files immediately before deleting the option files.
  * File permissions (main session finding of 2026-10-11, verified with `icacls` and `Win32_Service.StartName`): `C:\tools`, `C:\tools\mysql` and `C:\tools\mysql\current\bin` inherit "Authenticated Users: Modify" from `C:\`, and `C:\ProgramData\MySQL` inherits "Users: write data, append data, write attributes" (container inherit) from `C:\ProgramData`. The service runs as `LocalSystem` ("The default user for running the MySQL service is LocalSystem", MySQL Reference Manual, "Starting MySQL as a Windows Service"), so any local user could replace `mysqld.exe` and gain system rights at the next service start (local privilege escalation). A first fix that hardened these folders after the installation was not reliable (security re-check of 2026-10-11, H-1 to H-3: ordinary users can rename `C:\tools`, `icacls /grant:r` keeps foreign explicit permissions, and `icacls /t` may follow junctions) and was replaced by the layout below.
  * Layout of the local installation (project owner, 2026-10-11; replaces the later hardening of `C:\tools\mysql` and `C:\ProgramData\MySQL`): before `choco install mysql`, the script creates `C:\LEG-MySQL` with the subfolders `server` and `data` (no spaces, because the package registers the service with an unquoted path). Each folder is created with its security descriptor set at creation (`DirectoryInfo.Create(DirectorySecurity)` in .NET Framework, `FileSystemAclExtensions.Create` in .NET): inheritance from the parent blocked, full control for Administrators (`S-1-5-32-544`) and SYSTEM (`S-1-5-18`), read and execute for Users (`S-1-5-32-545`) on `server` only, owner Administrators. The script stops if `C:\LEG-MySQL` exists. It writes `server\mysql\current\my.ini` (`basedir`, `datadir=C:/LEG-MySQL/data/mysql/data`, `port`, `bind-address=127.0.0.1`, `mysqlx=OFF`) before the installation and installs with the package parameters `/installLocation:C:\LEG-MySQL\server /dataLocation:C:\LEG-MySQL\data`. The package installs to `<installLocation>\mysql\current`, writes `my.ini` only if it does not exist, creates `<dataLocation>\mysql\data`, initialises it with `mysqld --defaults-file=<that my.ini> --initialize-insecure`, registers the service with `mysqld --install MySQL` and starts it (`tools\chocolateyInstall.ps1` of mysql 9.7.2 on the development machine; the parameters are listed on https://community.chocolatey.org/packages/mysql). The server therefore listens only on `127.0.0.1` with X Plugin off from its first start. The script stops if one of the global option files `%WINDIR%\my.ini`, `%WINDIR%\my.cnf`, `C:\my.ini` or `C:\my.cnf` exists, because mysqld reads them before `BASEDIR\my.ini` (MySQL Reference Manual, "Using Option Files").
  * Owner (security re-check of 2026-10-11): the owner of objects created by members of the Administrators group depends on the policy "System objects: Default owner for objects created by members of the Administrators group" (values "Administrators group" or "Object creator"); its effective default on Windows 10 and 11 was not found stated on Microsoft Learn. If the installing user became the owner, a non-elevated process of that user could change the permissions. After writing `my.ini` and again after the installation, the script therefore enumerates `C:\LEG-MySQL` without following reparse points (any reparse point fails before anything is changed), checks each item again and sets its owner to Administrators with `ObjectSecurity.SetOwner` and `Set-Acl` (Microsoft Learn). Files that mysqld creates later while running as `LocalSystem` (tables, logs, `mysqld-auto.cnf`) get the owner of the service's token; which owner that is was not verified. They inherit the protected permissions of `data` (Administrators and SYSTEM only) in any case, and the allowlist check runs only during the setup.
  * Verification after the installation (allowlist): `my.ini` is unchanged and `mysql.ibd` exists in the data directory; every file and folder below `C:\LEG-MySQL` is enumerated without following reparse points (each reparse point fails), its owner must be Administrators or SYSTEM, and every allow entry must be Administrators or SYSTEM, or Users without any write right (generic rights count as write rights) and only below `server`; `C:\LEG-MySQL` may contain only `server` and `data`. Any violation fails into the existing failure path (service stopped and disabled).
  * `-RemoveFailedInstallation` handles both layouts: the service program must lie under `C:\LEG-MySQL\server\mysql` or `<tools>\mysql` with no other option file; `C:\LEG-MySQL` may contain only `server` and `data`, `<tools>\mysql` only `current` and `mysql-*-winx64` (other content of `<tools>` is never touched); `C:\ProgramData\MySQL\data` must look like a MySQL data directory. Everything is checked before the typed confirmation and again immediately before the removal; the trees are deleted with `[IO.Directory]::Delete(path, true)`, which "does not recurse through the reparse point". The removal also deletes the MySQL `bin` folders from the user `PATH` (the package adds them with `Install-ChocolateyPath`, default `PathType` User), because ordinary users can create folders directly below `C:\` (`Authenticated Users: (AD)` on `C:\`, verified with `icacls`) and could otherwise place programs where `PATH` points.
  * Security re-check of 2026-10-11 (M-A, L-B to L-I): before a tree that ordinary users may have been able to change is deleted (`<tools>\mysql`, `C:\ProgramData\MySQL\data`, or a `C:\LEG-MySQL` the script did not create), it is locked down top-down without following reparse points (per folder: fail on a reparse point, owner Administrators, protected DACL with Administrators and SYSTEM only, then its entries), verified with the allowlist (no Users entries) and checked again, then deleted; a foreign owner of `C:\LEG-MySQL` is reported before the confirmation. `server\mysql`, `current` and `my.ini` are created with their security descriptor and owner set at creation (`FileSystemAclExtensions.Create` for files in .NET, the `FileStream` constructor with `FileSecurity` in .NET Framework), and `my.ini` is compared again right before `choco install`. After the installation, the service is stopped while the owners are set and the allowlist is checked, and the setup fails if `BASEDIR\my.cnf` or `DATADIR\mysqld-auto.cnf` exists ("Even with --defaults-file, mysqld reads mysqld-auto.cnf"). The tools root of the earlier layout is the machine-wide `ChocolateyToolsLocation` or exactly `C:\tools`, never a process or user value. The script removes the earlier `PATH` entry `C:\tools\mysql\current\bin` from `HKCU\Environment` in every run, reading the value with `RegistryValueOptions.DoNotExpandEnvironmentNames` and writing it back with its original kind (the user `PATH` on the development machine is `REG_EXPAND_SZ`); MySQL entries in the machine `PATH` are only reported; a new terminal is needed to see the change (no broadcast). The mysql client is called with `--defaults-file=<temporary option file>` ("Read only the given option file") or `--no-defaults`; because clients still read `%APPDATA%\MySQL\.mylogin.cnf`, the script stops if that file exists (MySQL Reference Manual, "Command-Line Options that Affect Option-File Handling").
  * Security re-check of 2026-10-11 (N-1, decision of the main session, option c): `Lock-DirectoryTree` is removed, because `Set-Acl` works by name and could follow a swapped junction, and setting an inheritable DACL propagates into existing children. The script no longer removes the earlier layout (`<tools>\mysql`, `C:\ProgramData\MySQL`) or a `C:\LEG-MySQL` that fails the allowlist; it changes nothing and prints the manual commands (see the M-1 update below), to be run in an elevated PowerShell while no other user is signed in. `-RemoveFailedInstallation` removes automatically only a `C:\LEG-MySQL` that passes the allowlist (it was created protected, so ordinary users cannot have changed it), after the typed confirmation, with the service stop, `choco uninstall`, `sc.exe delete` and `PATH` cleanup as before. Residual risk accepted for the one-time manual cleanup on the developer's own machine: a junction planted inside the legacy folders is not followed by `Directory.Delete`, but the cleanup relies on no other user acting at the same time. The legacy tools root is the machine-wide `ChocolateyToolsLocation` or `C:\tools`.
  * Security re-check of 2026-10-11 (M-1, L-a to L-c): the earlier layout is detected by `<tools>\mysql` and `C:\ProgramData\MySQL\data` (not the parent, because MySQL's own installer uses `C:\ProgramData\MySQL\MySQL Server <version>`). The printed commands delete `C:\ProgramData\MySQL\data` recursively and the parent only non-recursively, which fails if it is not empty (tested with a mocked second installation). Service commands are printed only for the service of the earlier layout; for another service, the script warns. Before printing delete commands, the script checks that the tools root, `<tools>\mysql`, `C:\ProgramData\MySQL`, its `data` folder and (if present) `C:\LEG-MySQL` are not reparse points, and prints only a warning otherwise; the printed block starts with the same check for the user. The manual cleanup is run while no other user is signed in and no scheduled tasks or services of other users run.
  * Security re-check of 2026-10-11 (L-1, L-2, L-4): before the first client call, the script removes `MYSQL_TEST_LOGIN_FILE`, `MYSQL_HOST`, `MYSQL_TCP_PORT`, `MYSQL_PWD`, `MYSQL_UNIX_PORT`, `MYSQL_HOME`, `MYSQL_GROUP_SUFFIX`, `LIBMYSQL_PLUGIN_DIR`, `LIBMYSQL_PLUGINS` and `LIBMYSQL_ENABLE_CLEARTEXT_PLUGIN` from its own process (MySQL Reference Manual, "Environment Variables"; values are never printed), and passes `--protocol=TCP --host=127.0.0.1 --port=3306` on the command line, which takes "precedence over values specified in option files and environment variables". After the root password is set, `mysql.user` may contain only the reserved accounts `root`, `mysql.sys`, `mysql.session` and `mysql.infoschema` (all `@localhost`; MySQL Reference Manual, "Reserved Accounts"); the application account is created afterwards. `PATH` cleanup and user environment variables apply to the account that runs the elevated script (`HKCU`); the script warns if it differs from the console user (`Win32_ComputerSystem.UserName`).
  * Residual risks and open points of the local installation: after the initialisation, `root` has no password until the script sets it a few seconds later; the server listens only on the loopback interface during that time, so only processes on the same computer can use it. The service runs as `LocalSystem` (MySQL's default); running it with MySQL's documented `--local-service` option (`LocalService` account) is an open point, because the package installs the service itself. Folders created by ordinary users below `C:\` (e.g. a stale `C:\tools`) are outside the scope of the script. The creation with owner Administrators and the installation itself were not run by the AI (they need an elevated PowerShell); the helper functions were tested without elevation.
  * The Python step is skipped if the Chocolatey package `python314` is already installed (`choco list --exact --limit-output`, which lists only local packages from Chocolatey 2 on; the script requires Chocolatey 2).
  * The helper functions are tested by `scripts/local/test-setup-dev-windows.ps1`, which parses the script and loads only its function definitions and constants; the setup itself was not run by the AI.
* Open: licence of the public repository (decided in D-029).
* Sources: https://community.chocolatey.org/packages/mysql, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/processthreadsapi/nf-processthreadsapi-createprocessw (order of interpretation of an unquoted path with spaces), https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.io.directory.delete (reparse points), https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/netsecurity/get-netfirewallprofile, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.io.filesystemaclextensions.create, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.security.accesscontrol.objectsecurity.setowner, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/microsoft.win32.registryvalueoptions, https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/9.7/en/option-file-options.html, https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/9.7/en/environment-variables.html, https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/9.7/en/reserved-accounts.html, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.security/set-acl, https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/9.7/en/option-files.html (order of option files on Windows; "/" in Windows path names), Chocolatey helper `Get-ToolsLocation.ps1` and package script `tools\chocolateyInstall.ps1` of `mysql` 9.7.2 as installed on the development machine (checked on 2026-10-11), https://dev.mysql.com/doc/relnotes/mysql/8.4/en/, https://dev.mysql.com/doc/relnotes/mysql/9.7/en/, https://dev.mysql.com/doc/relnotes/mysql/9.7/en/news-9-7-0.html, https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/9.7/en/mysql-releases.html, https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/9.7/en/x-plugin-options-system-variables.html (`--mysqlx`, `mysqlx_bind_address`, `mysqlx_port`; same values in the 8.4 manual), https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/9.7/en/plugin-loading.html ("`--plugin_name=OFF` Tells the server to disable the plugin"; dashes and underscores are interchangeable), https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/9.7/en/server-system-variables.html (`bind_address`), https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/9.7/en/option-files.html, https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/9.7/en/privileges-provided.html, https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/9.7/en/grant.html, https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.0/topics/testing/overview/, https://docs.github.com/en/actions/security-for-github-actions/security-guides/security-hardening-for-github-actions (checked on 2026-10-11)

## D-028: Baseline Settings of the Django Project Skeleton

* Date: 2026-10-11
* Status: Decided. The Content Security Policy and local email delivery were decided by the project owner on 2026-10-11. On 2026-10-12 the project owner delegated the remaining parts to established practice with priority on security; the main session reviewed them against the sources listed below (checked on 2026-10-11, not re-fetched on 2026-10-12) and kept them unchanged.
* Context: README "Current Status", next step: create the Django project skeleton. `docs/SECURITY.md` (sections 3 and 6) requires secure defaults, but leaves several details of the settings open.
* Decision:
  * The environment variables are validated in one place (`leg_platform/environment.py`); invalid or unsafe values prevent the start. Beyond `APP_ENV`, `DJANGO_DEBUG` and `EMAIL_MODE`: outside `APP_ENV=local`, `DJANGO_SECRET_KEY` and every fallback key must meet the criteria of Django's deployment check `security.W009` (at least 50 characters, at least 5 unique characters, no `django-insecure-` prefix), `DJANGO_ALLOWED_HOSTS` must be set, and `DJANGO_CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` accepts only `https://` origins. Wildcards (`*`, leading `.`) are rejected in `DJANGO_ALLOWED_HOSTS` and `DJANGO_CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` in all environments.
  * HSTS in stage and production with a default of one year (`DJANGO_SECURE_HSTS_SECONDS`, can be lowered for testing but not disabled).
  * `SECURE_HSTS_INCLUDE_SUBDOMAINS` is off by default and can be enabled by the operator with `DJANGO_SECURE_HSTS_INCLUDE_SUBDOMAINS=True`: the policy would also apply to every host below the application's host name, which only the operator can assess. The deployment warning `security.W005` is silenced only while it is off (security review of 2026-10-11, L-1).
  * `SECURE_HSTS_PRELOAD` is never set and `security.W021` is always silenced. Preloading puts the domain into lists built into browsers; removal depends on the list maintainers and on browser releases, so it is effectively irreversible for an operator and not offered as a setting. Silencing keeps `check --deploy --fail-level WARNING` meaningful for every other warning.
  * `EMAIL_MODE` per `APP_ENV` (`docs/SECURITY.md`, sections 8 and 11): `local` allows `console` (its default) and `allowlist`, `stage` requires `allowlist`, `production` requires `graph`; outside `local`, a missing `EMAIL_MODE` prevents the start (security review of 2026-10-11, M-1).
  * Local email (project owner, 2026-10-11, to test Microsoft Graph delivery locally): `allowlist` is allowed locally; `graph` (unrestricted) stays production-only. Local `allowlist` delivery requires a dedicated development app registration and sender mailbox whose credentials are separate from stage and production (D-020, `docs/SECURITY.md`, section 7). The allowed local recipient addresses are kept only in the user environment variable `EMAIL_ALLOWED_RECIPIENTS` on the developer machine, never in the repository. `docs/SECURITY.md` sections 8 and 11 were aligned accordingly, which resolves the earlier contradiction between them ("controlled by default" versus "restricted to test output"); the project owner consented to this change.
  * Automated tests never send real email: Django's test runner sets the locmem backend (`django/test/utils.py`: `EMAIL_BACKEND` in 6.0.9, every alias of `MAILERS` in 6.1.2), verified by a test in both versions. Tests and examples use only addresses under reserved domains (RFC 2606: `example.com`, `example.net`, `example.org`, `.test`, `.invalid`); `leg_platform/tests/test_repository.py` scans the text files of the repository (not `.env`) for other addresses.
  * `allowlist` with an empty `EMAIL_ALLOWED_RECIPIENTS` refuses the start in every environment (re-review of 2026-10-11, N-5; security review of 2026-10-11, L-3). Each entry must be exactly one complete address (exactly one `@`, a domain with at least two labels, no white space, wildcards or domain-only entries); entries are stored in lower case. Error messages name only the position of an invalid entry, never an address. The Microsoft Graph backend must still deliver only to these addresses (to be tested when it is built).
  * `DEFAULT_EXCEPTION_REPORTER_FILTER = "leg_platform.debug.ExceptionReporterFilter"`: a subclass of Django's `SafeExceptionReporterFilter` whose `hidden_settings` pattern adds `RECIPIENTS` to Django's default (`API|AUTH|TOKEN|KEY|SECRET|PASS|SIGNATURE|HTTP_COOKIE`, identical in 6.0.9 and 6.1.2), so that debug pages and error reports mask `EMAIL_ALLOWED_RECIPIENTS` in the settings and in `request.META` (Django documentation, "How to manage error reporting": `hidden_settings` matches "settings and `request.META` values considered as sensitive"). The setting keeps its name, which matches the environment variable.
  * Numeric values (`DB_PORT`, `DJANGO_SECURE_HSTS_SECONDS`) accept only ASCII digits; ports must be in the range 1 to 65535, also in `DJANGO_CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS`.
  * `CSRF_COOKIE_HTTPONLY = True`: no JavaScript reads the CSRF cookie. Django's documentation states that this "doesn't offer any practical protection" but is "sometimes required by security auditors"; `docs/SECURITY.md` (section 3) requires `HttpOnly` where possible. Scripts that need the token read it from the form field rendered by `{% csrf_token %}` (Django documentation "How to use Django's CSRF protection").
  * `SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER` is set only through `DJANGO_SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER`, never by default.
  * Email delivery via Microsoft Graph is not implemented yet. In the modes `allowlist` and `graph`, a placeholder backend (`leg_platform.mail.GraphNotImplementedEmailBackend`) raises an error on every send attempt, so that email is never dropped silently and never sent through Django's default SMTP backend or to the console. Django 6.1 deprecates `EMAIL_BACKEND` in favour of `MAILERS`, which Django 6.0 does not know; the settings set `MAILERS` on Django 6.1 and later and `EMAIL_BACKEND` otherwise.
  * Database connection (MariaDB through Django's MySQL backend since 2026-10-11, D-030): `isolation_level` "read committed" (Django's default, set explicitly; MariaDB's own default is `REPEATABLE-READ`), `charset` utf8mb4, test database with utf8mb4 and `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` (until 2026-10-11 `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci`, which MariaDB has only as an alias from 11.4.5 on and 10.11 not at all). `sql_mode` is set for every connection with `OPTIONS["init_command"]` (Django documentation, "Setting sql_mode"; security review of 2026-10-11, L-6) to MariaDB's documented default `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES,ERROR_FOR_DIVISION_BY_ZERO,NO_AUTO_CREATE_USER,NO_ENGINE_SUBSTITUTION` (MariaDB documentation, "Server System Variables"), so that a changed server default cannot weaken it; Django's check `mysql.W002` (run in CI with `--database default`) and tests (tag `mariadb`) verify strict mode and the exact session value. Collations of identifier columns: D-031.
  * Logging: warnings and errors to standard error; no email to `ADMINS`.
* Verified facts:
  * Django's MySQL notes (which apply to MariaDB, D-030): "Django works best with and defaults to read committed rather than MySQL's default, repeatable read. Data loss is possible with repeatable read."; "The default value of the `sql_mode` option contains `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES`." MariaDB's default for `transaction_isolation` is also `REPEATABLE-READ` (MariaDB documentation, "Server System Variables"). Django connects with `charset` utf8mb4 by default (source `django/db/backends/mysql/base.py`, 5.2.18 and 6.1.2).
  * Django's SecurityMiddleware documentation: "it's a good idea to first use a small value for testing, for example, `SECURE_HSTS_SECONDS = 3600` [...] (31536000 seconds, i.e. 1 year, is common)"; "The HSTS policy applies to your entire domain".
  * Django's settings reference on `SECURE_HSTS_INCLUDE_SUBDOMAINS`: "Setting this incorrectly can irreversibly (for the value of `SECURE_HSTS_SECONDS`) break your site."
  * Django's settings reference on `CSRF_COOKIE_HTTPONLY`: "If you enable this and need to send the value of the CSRF token with an AJAX request, your JavaScript must pull the value from a hidden CSRF token form input instead of from the cookie."
  * Django 6.1 release notes: `EMAIL_BACKEND` and related settings "are deprecated. Replace them with a `MAILERS` configuration"; new deployment check `mail.E001` against development-only backends in `MAILERS`; "The check management command now supplies all databases if not specified."
  * Django 6.0 and later provide `django.middleware.csp` and `SECURE_CSP` (present in 6.1.2); Django 5.2.18 has no built-in Content Security Policy support (no `django/middleware/csp.py`). `django/middleware/csp.py` is identical in 6.0.9 and 6.1.2.
  * None of Django's password validators sets a maximum length (source `django/contrib/auth/password_validation.py`, 6.1.2).
* Rationale: fail early and closed on configuration errors; operators without in-depth IT knowledge get secure defaults (`docs/SECURITY.md`, section 1).
* Content Security Policy (project owner, 2026-10-11: use Django's built-in support; minimum raised to Django 6.0, D-026):
  * `django.middleware.csp.ContentSecurityPolicyMiddleware` directly after `SecurityMiddleware`, and in all environments `SECURE_CSP = {"default-src": [CSP.SELF], "script-src": [CSP.SELF], "style-src": [CSP.SELF], "img-src": [CSP.SELF], "font-src": [CSP.SELF], "object-src": [CSP.NONE], "base-uri": [CSP.NONE], "form-action": [CSP.SELF], "frame-ancestors": [CSP.NONE]}`, rendered as `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; img-src 'self'; font-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'`. No `'unsafe-inline'`, no nonces, no report-only policy. `X-Frame-Options: DENY` stays as a fallback for browsers without `frame-ancestors`.
  * Directive names: W3C Content Security Policy Level 3 (`default-src`, `script-src`, `style-src`, `img-src`, `font-src` and `object-src` are fetch directives; `base-uri` is a document directive; `form-action` and `frame-ancestors` are navigation directives; "frame-ancestors will not fall back to the default-src directive's value"). Value constants: `django.utils.csp.CSP` (`SELF` = `'self'`, `NONE` = `'none'`); Django's documentation uses directive names as string keys, e.g. `"default-src": [CSP.SELF]`.
  * Django admin under this policy (checked in the installed sources of 6.0.9 and 6.1.2): the admin templates contain no inline `<script>` (every `<script>` has a `src`; configuration is passed in `data-` attributes), no `style` attributes, no event handler attributes and no `javascript:` URLs; the admin CSS contains no `data:` or external URLs; the admin JavaScript outside `vendor/` contains no `eval`, `new Function` or `setAttribute("style")`. One exception: `admin/change_list.html` contains an inline `<style>` block (`#changelist table thead th:first-child {width: inherit}`) that is rendered only when a change list shows no actions (`actions_on_top` and `actions_on_bottom` both off). Django 6.1 adds `{% csp_nonce_attr %}` to it, Django 6.0 does not; without a nonce in `style-src`, browsers block it in both versions. The effect is cosmetic (width of the first column); the policy is not relaxed for it. A test verifies that the admin login page contains no inline code and carries the header. The admin was not checked in a browser.
  * Django's CSRF failure page (`csrf_403.html`) has an inline `<style>`; under this policy it is displayed without its styles (cosmetic). Django's debug pages (only with `DEBUG=True`, local) disable the policy for themselves with `csp_override({})`.
  * Tests: the header on responses with production settings (including a 500 error response) and on the admin login page with the local settings of the test run, the settings in a separate process with `APP_ENV=production`, and the absence of `'unsafe-inline'`, `'unsafe-eval'`, `'unsafe-hashes'`, nonces, wildcards and `data:`.
* Security implications: Encrypted transport to the database in stage and production (`docs/SECURITY.md`, section 9) is not configured yet and depends on the hosting (open).
* Alternatives: `SECURE_HSTS_INCLUDE_SUBDOMAINS = True` by default (rejected: could break other hosts of the operator's domain; left to the operator); silencing `mail.E001` instead of a placeholder backend (rejected: it would hide that production cannot send email); Django's dummy backend (rejected: drops email silently).
* Sources (checked on 2026-10-11): https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/5.2/ref/databases/, https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/5.2/ref/middleware/, https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/5.2/ref/settings/ (`SECURE_HSTS_INCLUDE_SUBDOMAINS`, `CSRF_COOKIE_HTTPONLY`, `CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS`), https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/5.2/howto/csrf/, https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.1/releases/6.1/, https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/5.2/howto/deployment/checklist/, installed source of Django 6.1.2 and 5.2.18
* Sources (local email, checked on 2026-10-11): https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc2606 (reserved top-level domains `.test`, `.example`, `.invalid`, `.localhost` and second-level domains `example.com`, `example.net`, `example.org`), installed `django/test/utils.py` of Django 6.0.9 and 6.1.2
* Sources (Content Security Policy, checked on 2026-10-11): https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.0/ref/csp/, https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.0/howto/csp/, https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.0/releases/6.0/, https://www.w3.org/TR/CSP3/ (Working Draft, 16 September 2026), installed sources of Django 6.0.9 and 6.1.2 (`django/middleware/csp.py`, `django/utils/csp.py`, `django/views/debug.py`, `django/contrib/admin/templates/`, `django/contrib/admin/static/`)
* Consequences: application templates must not use inline scripts, inline styles or style attributes; a page that would need a relaxation requires a decision and must not relax the global policy; the placeholder email backend is replaced by the Microsoft Graph backend (D-006).

## D-029: Licence

* Date: 2026-10-11
* Status: Decided (project owner, 2026-10-11)
* Context: The repository is public (D-027). Without a licence, the default copyright applies and nobody may reuse the code. The project owner asked for a licence chosen according to the packages used.
* Decision:
  * LEG-Platform is licensed under the BSD 3-Clause License (SPDX identifier `BSD-3-Clause`), the licence of Django.
  * Copyright line: "Copyright (c) 2026, the LEG-Platform contributors".
  * `LICENSE` contains the licence text of the SPDX License List (https://spdx.org/licenses/BSD-3-Clause.json, field `licenseText`, copied verbatim except for the copyright line, which replaces the template line "Copyright (c) <year> <owner>.").
  * `pyproject.toml` declares `license = "BSD-3-Clause"` and `license-files = ["LICENSE"]` (PEP 639).
  * `docs/THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` lists the licences of all dependencies. Bundled assets (Tabler, Tabler Icons, fonts; D-024) ship their licence texts when they are added.
* Verified facts:
  * Licences in the package metadata of the installed versions (Python 3.14.8, 2026-10-11): Django BSD-3-Clause; django-axes MIT; django-otp Unlicense; cryptography Apache-2.0 OR BSD-3-Clause; mysqlclient GPL-2.0-or-later; msal MIT; segno BSD (3-clause licence text); transitive: asgiref BSD-3-Clause, sqlparse BSD (3-clause licence text), requests Apache-2.0, PyJWT MIT, urllib3 MIT, charset-normalizer MIT, idna BSD-3-Clause, certifi MPL-2.0, cffi MIT-0, pycparser BSD-3-Clause, tzdata Apache-2.0. Tabler and Tabler Icons are MIT-licensed, the fonts SIL OFL 1.1 (D-024).
  * Free Software Foundation, "Various Licenses and Comments about Them": Modified BSD license "compatible with the GNU GPL"; Expat "compatible with the GNU GPL"; Apache License 2.0 "compatible with version 3 of the GNU GPL" and "not compatible with GPL version 2"; MPL 2.0 "Section 3.3 provides indirect compatibility"; Unlicense "compatible with the GNU GPL".
  * setuptools supports SPDX licence expressions in `project.license` and `project.license-files` from version 77.0.0; the project requires setuptools ≥ 84.0.0. A wheel built locally contains `Metadata-Version: 2.4`, `License-Expression: BSD-3-Clause` and `License-File: LICENSE`.
* Rationale: BSD-3-Clause is the licence of the main framework, is permissive for municipalities that run or adapt their own instance (D-013), and is GPL-compatible, so that LEG-Platform can be combined with `mysqlclient` (GPL-2.0-or-later). Following the FSF list, a combined work containing `mysqlclient` and Apache-2.0 code can only be distributed under GPL-3.0-or-later; the GPL's conditions apply when such a work is conveyed to others (GPLv3, section 2: "You may make, run and propagate covered works that you do not convey, without conditions so long as your license otherwise remains in force"). The repository conveys only the source code of LEG-Platform.
* Security review of 2026-10-11 (L-5): the Windows wheels of `mysqlclient` 2.3.0 statically link MariaDB Connector/C (LGPL-2.1; FSF: "compatible with GPLv2 and GPLv3"); listed in `docs/THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`.
* Security implications: none.
* Legal note: this decision is not legal advice and was not reviewed by a lawyer. The dependencies are installed separately and are not distributed with the source code; a distribution that bundles them (e.g. a container image) must comply with their licences, in particular the GPL of `mysqlclient`.
* Alternatives: no licence (rejected: no reuse permitted); a copyleft licence such as GPL-3.0-or-later (not chosen: the project owner asked for a licence chosen according to the packages used; the main framework uses BSD-3-Clause); MIT (equally permissive; BSD-3-Clause chosen for consistency with Django).
* Sources (checked on 2026-10-11): https://spdx.org/licenses/BSD-3-Clause.html, https://spdx.org/licenses/BSD-3-Clause.json, https://www.gnu.org/licenses/license-list.en.html, https://www.gnu.org/licenses/gpl-3.0.en.html, https://github.com/mariadb-corporation/mariadb-connector-c, https://setuptools.pypa.io/en/latest/userguide/pyproject_config.html, https://peps.python.org/pep-0639/, installed package metadata (`importlib.metadata`) and PyPI package pages
* Consequences: new dependencies and bundled assets are added to `docs/THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` with their licences.

## D-030: MariaDB Instead of MySQL

* Date: 2026-10-11
* Status: Decided (project owner, 2026-10-11)
* Context: Production of the Ittigen instance will run on an Infomaniak "VPS Lite 1" (D-008). The project owner was advised that MariaDB 12.3 is less resource-intensive for this application and largely compatible with MySQL. This is a recommendation the project owner received; it was not verified (no official source, no measurement). The first local MySQL installation had failed (D-027).
* Decision:
  * The database is MariaDB, used through Django's MySQL backend with `mysqlclient` (D-002). MySQL is no longer supported; supporting it again requires a new decision.
  * Minimum version MariaDB 10.11; used version: the newest long-term support release, 12.3 (latest patch 12.3.3) (D-015). Continuous integration tests Python 3.12, Django 6.0 and MariaDB 10.11 (minimum) and Python 3.14, Django 6.1 and MariaDB 12.3 (latest) with the official Docker image `mariadb`, pinned by digest; the database tests have the tag `mariadb`.
  * Settings: the collation of the test database is `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` (D-028), and `sql_mode` is set for every connection with `init_command` to MariaDB's documented default (D-028; security review of 2026-10-11, L-6). Collations of identifier columns: D-031.
  * Local development (replaces the MySQL parts of D-027): `scripts/local/setup-dev-windows.ps1` installs the Chocolatey package `mariadb.install` 12.3.3 below `%ProgramFiles%` with the additional MSI property `SKIPNETWORKING=1`, so that the server first listens on no TCP port but only on the named pipe of its service. It then verifies the installation, restricts the data directory, hardens the server over the named pipe, switches it to TCP on `127.0.0.1:3306` only with the named pipe disabled, and creates the application account (details in `docs/INSTALLATION.md`). The root password is not passed to the MSI, because it would appear in the command line and in Chocolatey's log.
  * The script refuses to run while the earlier MySQL installation (`C:\tools\mysql`, `C:\ProgramData\MySQL\data`, the package `mysql`), any service named `MySQL` or `MariaDB`, or a MariaDB installation exists, and prints the manual commands for the earlier MySQL installation (unchanged from D-027). With `-RemoveFailedInstallation`, it removes only a MariaDB installation of this script (exactly one MariaDB product below `%ProgramFiles%`, installed with the package `mariadb.install`, service program in its `bin` folder) after a typed confirmation: stop and disable the service, uninstall the MSI by its ProductCode, remove the Chocolatey package records and the `bin` entry of the machine `PATH`, then delete leftovers only if they belong to the recorded program folder.
* Continuous integration (security review of 2026-10-11, L-5; re-check of 2026-10-12, L-d): a step creates the database `leg_platform` with `utf8mb4` and `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` (D-031) instead of the image's `MARIADB_DATABASE`, and `leg_platform@'%'` with exactly the privileges of the local application account on `leg\_platform` and `test\_leg\_platform` (checked in `mysql.db`), and the checks and tests run with this account instead of `root`.
* Verified facts:
  * MariaDB maintenance policy (https://mariadb.org/about/, checked by the main session on 2026-10-11): one long-term support release per year; community binaries for 3 years after general availability; 12.3 GA 28 May 2026, community end of life 12 June 2029; 11.4 until 29 May 2029; 10.11 until 16 February 2028; 10.6 until 6 July 2026 (past). "these dates are public policy and are not legally binding". The download service (https://downloads.mariadb.org/rest-api/mariadb/, checked on 2026-10-11) lists 12.3 as "Stable", "Long Term Support", end of life 2029-06-12, and the latest releases 12.3.3 (2026-08-22) and 10.11.19; it also lists the rolling releases 13.0 (Stable) and 13.1 (RC), which are not long-term support releases. Contradiction: for 11.8 the download service gives 2030-02-13, the maintenance policy as read by the main session 4 June 2028; this does not affect the decision.
  * Django 6.0: "Django supports MariaDB 10.6 and higher."; "To use MariaDB, use the MySQL backend"; Django 6.1: "Django supports MariaDB 10.11 and higher." (https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.0/ref/databases/, 6.1 checked by the main session). The installed Django 6.1.2 returns `(10, 11)` as `minimum_database_version` for MariaDB (`django/db/backends/mysql/features.py`).
  * Collations: "The UCA-14.0.0 collations were added in MariaDB 10.10.1"; the default collation of utf8mb4 is `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` from MariaDB 11.5 on; `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` exists only as an "Alias for utf8mb4_uca1400_nopad_ai_ci" from 11.4.5 on (MariaDB documentation, "Supported Character Sets and Collations"; MDEV-35256). `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` is therefore available in 10.11 and 12.3.
  * Defaults (MariaDB documentation, "Server System Variables"): `sql_mode` `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES,ERROR_FOR_DIVISION_BY_ZERO,NO_AUTO_CREATE_USER,NO_ENGINE_SUBSTITUTION`; `transaction_isolation` `REPEATABLE-READ` (`tx_isolation` "deprecated in MariaDB 11.1 and replaced by transaction_isolation"); `skip_networking` 0; `named_pipe` "On Windows systems, determines whether connections over named pipes are permitted", default `OFF`; `socket` default `MySQL` on Windows.
  * Unique indexes permit multiple `NULL` values (D-016).
  * Docker Hub, official image `library/mariadb` (https://hub.docker.com/v2/repositories/library/mariadb/tags/<tag>, 2026-10-11): `10.11` = `10.11.19`, digest `sha256:7db29378...257f2`; `12.3` = `12.3.3`, digest `sha256:2bdff153...a97c4` (full digests in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`). Root password variable `MARIADB_ROOT_PASSWORD`; `MARIADB_DATABASE` (image description). Health check `healthcheck.sh --connect --innodb_initialized`: `--connect` is "Active when an external user can connect to the TCP port of MariaDB Server", `--innodb_initialized` "True when InnoDB has completed initializing" (MariaDB documentation, "Using Healthcheck.sh").
  * Chocolatey (https://docs.chocolatey.org/en-us/choco/commands/install/): "Install args are installer arguments, appended to the silentArgs in the package for the installer itself"; `--override-arguments` "Should install arguments be used exclusively without appending" (default false). The package `mariadb.install` 12.3.3 (read by the main session from the package) installs `mariadb-12.3.3-winx64.msi` with `SERVICENAME=MySQL /qn /norestart /l*v "<log>"`, reads `InstallLocation` from the uninstall registry key (DisplayName `MariaDB *`), adds `<InstallLocation>\bin` to the machine `PATH` and runs `NET START MySQL`. SHA-256 of `mariadb-12.3.3-winx64.msi` according to the download service: `811a38a862c1c55325b6ba8a757b381923e51dee4fe3cde95887b0ae2490c02d`.
  * MSI and `mariadb-install-db.exe` (MariaDB documentation "Installing MariaDB MSI Packages on Windows" and "Installing System Tables on Windows"; sources of the MariaDB server repository, branch 12.3: `win/packaging/extra.wxs.in`, `sql/mysql_install_db.cc`, `scripts/mariadb_system_tables_data.sql`, `scripts/mariadb_test_db.sql`, `sql/CMakeLists.txt`, `sql/handle_connections_win.cc`, `scripts/mysql_secure_installation.sh`; read on 2026-10-11):
    * A non-empty `SKIPNETWORKING` makes the MSI call `mariadb-install-db.exe` with `--skip-networking`, which writes `skip-networking=ON`, `named-pipe=ON` and `socket=<service name>` to `[mysqld]` and `protocol=pipe` to `[client]` of `my.ini` in the data directory. The root password is set only if `PASSWORD` is not empty; the MSI default for `DEFAULTUSER` is empty, so anonymous accounts are deleted; `FEEDBACK` (the plugin that sends usage data) is off unless set.
    * The service command line is `"<bin>\mysqld.exe" "--defaults-file=<datadir>\my.ini" "<service>"`; the service runs under the virtual account `NT SERVICE\<service>` ("Run service under virtual account NT SERVICE\service_name").
    * The data directory receives full access for the service account and read/write for the installing process in addition to what it inherits; the MSI additionally grants the logged-on user `GenericAll` on it; the subfolders created during the bootstrap receive an explicit copy of the data directory's DACL (`MARIADB_NEW_DIRECTORY_SDDL`). Below `%ProgramFiles%` it therefore inherits read access for Users, so the script restricts it.
    * The Windows bootstrap creates `root` without a password for `localhost`, the host name, `127.0.0.1` and `::1`, the locked account `mariadb.sys@localhost`, the database `test` and grants on `test` and `test\_%` to `PUBLIC` (all accounts), which would include `test_leg_platform`. The hardening follows `mariadb-secure-installation`: delete anonymous accounts and root accounts for other hosts than `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` and `::1`, drop `test` and delete its grants, `FLUSH PRIVILEGES`, then set the root password.
    * The named pipe is created with `FILE_FLAG_FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE`, a DACL that gives Everyone read and write "minus FILE_CREATE_PIPE_INSTANCE" and a low integrity label "so that it is possible to connect from any process"; `PIPE_REJECT_REMOTE_CLIENTS` is not set.
  * MariaDB does not read MySQL's `.mylogin.cnf` ("MariaDB and MariaDB Enterprise Server do not support this feature"); `--no-defaults` and `--defaults-file` "Must be given as the first option"; option files on Windows include `installdir\data\my.ini`, `MARIADB_HOME`/`MYSQL_HOME` (MariaDB documentation, "Configuring MariaDB with Option Files", "mariadb Command-Line Client", "MariaDB Environment Variables"; Connector/C sources `libmariadb/ma_default.c` and `ma_client_plugin.c.in` for `MARIADB_HOME`, `MARIADB_PLUGIN_DIR` and `LIBMYSQL_PLUGINS`).
  * The `REFERENCES` privilege is listed as "Unused" (MariaDB documentation, "GRANT"); it is no longer granted to the application account. Database names in `GRANT` "can contain % and _ wildcards"; the escaped form `\_` is the one `mariadb-secure-installation` uses for `test\_%`, and MDEV-39047 confirms that escaped names are stored escaped in `mysql.db` (it reports a truncation for names near 64 characters, which does not affect the short names used here).
  * MariaDB Server is licensed under GPL-2.0 (`COPYING` in the MariaDB server repository).
  * The MSI attaches a Windows Firewall exception to `mysqld.exe` ("<FirewallException ... Name='[ProductName]' Scope='any'", `win/packaging/CPackWixConfig.cmake`); the uninstall registry DisplayName is the MSI product name `MariaDB <major>.<minor> (<arch>)`, e.g. `MariaDB 12.3 (x64)` (`CPACK_WIX_PACKAGE_NAME`, `win/packaging/CMakeLists.txt`). The script disables enabled inbound allow rules for the installed server program (security review of 2026-10-11, L-7, and a finding of the developer while verifying L-7) and locates only the exact product name; the check of the active firewall store fails closed if it cannot be read (re-check of 2026-10-12, L-b). An upgrade or repair of the MSI may create or enable the rule again (installation guide).
  * Besides the visible uninstall entry named after the package name, the MSI registers a hidden uninstall entry under its ProductCode (`ARPSYSTEMCOMPONENT=1`) with the same DisplayName and InstallLocation (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/MariaDB/server/12.3/win/packaging/extra.wxs.in, branch 12.3, checked on 2026-10-12; `ARPSYSTEMCOMPONENT=1` hides the product from the list of installed programs: Microsoft Learn, "ARPSYSTEMCOMPONENT property", https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/msi/arpsystemcomponent; both entries observed on a development machine on 2026-10-12). Each visible entry with the same DisplayName and InstallLocation explains at most one hidden entry; any other hidden entry is counted, so a hidden-only installation is still detected.
  * Removal with `-RemoveFailedInstallation` (2026-10-12): `choco uninstall mariadb.install` failed with exit code 1619, because the package's `tools\chocolateyUninstall.ps1` (12.3.3) calls `Uninstall-ChocolateyPackage -FileType msi -File "<UninstallString>"` and the helper (Chocolatey 2.4.0, `helpers\functions\Uninstall-ChocolateyPackage.ps1`, read on the development machine) builds only `msiexec.exe /x <silentArgs>` for MSI files and ignores `-File` (observed log: `/x /quiet /qn /norestart` without a product). The script therefore takes the ProductCode from the key name of the one hidden uninstall entry with the same DisplayName and InstallLocation, runs `%SystemRoot%\System32\msiexec.exe /x <ProductCode> /qn /norestart` ("/x Uninstalls a product", Microsoft Learn, "Command-Line Options"; "/norestart ... never restarts", "Standard Installer Command-Line Options") and accepts 0 and 3010 ("A restart is required to complete the install. This message indicates success", Microsoft Learn, "MsiExec.exe and InstMsi.exe error messages"; after 3010 the script completes the removal and stops, asking for a restart before the new installation), then removes the package records with `choco uninstall <package> -y --no-progress -n --skip-autouninstaller` (`-n`: "Do not run chocolateyUninstall.ps1"; "A package with a failing uninstall can be removed with the `-n --skipautouninstaller` flags", Chocolatey documentation, "Uninstall"), also for the meta package `mariadb` (its scripts were not available to check), and removes exactly `<InstallLocation>\bin` from the machine `PATH`, which the package's install script adds and its skipped uninstall script would remove. Sources (checked on 2026-10-12): https://docs.chocolatey.org/en-us/choco/commands/uninstall/, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/msi/command-line-options, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/msi/standard-installer-command-line-options, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/msi/error-codes.
  * Bootstrap state on Windows (sources above, branch 12.3): `mysql.tables_priv` contains only `mariadb.sys@localhost` with `Select,Delete` on `mysql.global_priv`; `mysql.proxies_priv` contains `root` for `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1` and the host name with an empty proxied user; `mariadb.sys@localhost` is created with `"account_locked":true` in `mysql.global_priv.Priv` (`JSON_VALUE` returns a JSON `true` as `1` and `false` as `0`: `Json_engine_scan::check_and_get_value_scalar` in `sql/item_jsonfunc.cc`, tag `mariadb-12.3.3`, checked on 2026-10-12; the lock check therefore compares with `1` and also requires `JSON_TYPE` `BOOLEAN` (`Item_func_json_type::val_str`, same file), so that a number or string `1` does not count as locked (security review of 2026-10-12, L-1), after the first check with `'true'` failed on 12.3.3); `mysql.db` contains only the `test` grants; no rows are inserted into `columns_priv`, `procs_priv`, `roles_mapping`, `plugin`, `func` or `servers` (the plugin `auth_socket` is installed only when `auth_root_socket` is set, which `mariadb-install-db.exe` does not do); the `sys` schema objects have the definer `mariadb.sys@localhost`.
  * Information Schema `PROCESSLIST`: the documentation's example shows a background thread with USER "system user" and COMMAND "Daemon"; the script counts every other thread except its own (`CONNECTION_ID()`). Compound statements (`IF ... THEN ... END IF`) may be used outside stored programs "with the delimiter set appropriately" (MariaDB documentation, "Using Compound Statements Outside of Stored Programs"); `SIGNAL` makes the client stop with an error.
  * Server variables (MariaDB documentation, "Server System Variables"): `general_log` default 0, `slow_query_log` default 0, `log_output` default `FILE`, `general_log_file` default `host_name.log`; `shared_memory` is listed as "Windows only, determines whether the server permits shared memory connections", without a documented default and without a removal note (contradicts the server, see the next point). MariaDB has no persisted system variables, so a restart resets values changed with `SET GLOBAL`.
  * `shared_memory` is not present in MariaDB 12.3 (2026-10-12); `sql/handle_connections_win.cc` at tag `mariadb-12.3.3` creates only TCP and named pipe listeners, so an absent variable means no shared memory connections (security review of 2026-10-12, L-3; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/MariaDB/server/mariadb-12.3.3/sql/handle_connections_win.cc). A NULL value counts as a problem. Events are read from the table `mysql.event`, not from `information_schema.EVENTS`: with `event-scheduler=DISABLED`, `Events::init` returns before the event subsystem is initialised and `Events::fill_schema_events` then fails with error 1577 (`sql/events.cc`, tag `mariadb-12.3.3`, checked on 2026-10-12; observed after the restart on 12.3.3). In the server check (`CONCAT`), boolean variables are returned as the text `OFF`/`ON` (`sys_var::val_str_nolock` in `sql/set_var.cc`, tag `mariadb-12.3.3`, checked on 2026-10-12); the server check therefore maps these four variables to `OFF`/`ON` itself with `IF(... = 0, 'OFF', 'ON')` and requires exactly `OFF` (security review of 2026-10-12, L-1), after the first check against `0` failed on 12.3.3. The first run of the setup script with MariaDB 12.3.3 (Windows MSI) failed at the hardening checks over the named pipe with `ERROR 1193 (HY000) at line 21: Unknown system variable 'shared_memory'` (observed by the main session; lines 1 to 20 of the batch, including `@@GLOBAL.event_scheduler` and `@@GLOBAL.general_log`, ran without error). `sql/sys_vars.cc` of branch 12.3 (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/MariaDB/server/12.3/sql/sys_vars.cc, fetched 2026-10-12) defines `named_pipe` under `#ifdef _WIN32` but no `shared_memory` or `shared_memory_base_name`; a GitHub code search in MariaDB/server (default branch, main session) found no `shared_memory` in `sql/sys_vars.cc`, no `HAVE_SMEM` and no "shared-memory" in `sql/mysqld.cc`. The version that removed it was not verified. Contradiction: the documentation page "Server System Variables" (fetched 2026-10-12) still lists `shared_memory`; the server behaviour and the source take precedence. Consequences for the script: no statement references `@@GLOBAL.shared_memory`; the value is read from `information_schema.GLOBAL_VARIABLES` and only `OFF` or absent is accepted (the hardening check returns a problem row if the variable exists and is not `OFF`; the server check reads `COALESCE(..., 'ABSENT')` and refuses any other value); `shared-memory=OFF` is no longer written to `my.ini`, because an unknown option would very likely stop the server from starting (not verified; MariaDB 13.0 documents exit code 7 "unknown variable" for `--validate-config`, which 12.3 does not have); an initial `my.ini` that turns shared memory on is still refused, and `shared-memory` stays in the list of listener options removed from the server groups before the switch to TCP (fail-safe: a leftover line is removed). `information_schema.GLOBAL_VARIABLES` holds "the same information as displayed by" `SHOW GLOBAL VARIABLES`, and its example shows upper-case names (MariaDB documentation, "Information Schema GLOBAL_VARIABLES and SESSION_VARIABLES Tables", https://mariadb.com/docs/server/reference/system-tables/information-schema/information-schema-tables/information-schema-global_variables-and-session_variables-tables, checked 2026-10-12); `sql/sql_show.cc` of branch 12.3 (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/MariaDB/server/12.3/sql/sql_show.cc, checked 2026-10-12) writes upper-case names for Information Schema queries (`upper_case_names= lex->sql_command != SQLCOM_SHOW_VARIABLES` in `fill_variables`) and boolean variables as `ON` or `OFF` (`SHOW_MY_BOOL` in `get_one_variable`). All other variables the script reads with `@@GLOBAL` (`bind_address`, `skip_networking`, `named_pipe`, `port`, `general_log`, `slow_query_log`, `log_output`, `general_log_file`, `slow_query_log_file`, `event_scheduler`) and all options it writes to `my.ini` (`port`, `bind-address`, `skip-networking`, `named-pipe`, `event-scheduler`) are defined in `sql/sys_vars.cc` of branch 12.3 (`named_pipe` only on Windows, `event_scheduler` only with `HAVE_EVENT_SCHEDULER`).
  * Windows Installer runs an installation while its service accepts `SERVICE_ACCEPT_SHUTDOWN` (Microsoft Learn, "_MSIExecute Mutex"); `ServiceController.CanShutdown` reports this flag.
  * Django documentation "Setting sql_mode": "'init_command': \"SET sql_mode='STRICT_TRANS_TABLES'\"" in `OPTIONS`.
* Security implications:
  * Phase without a root password (security review of 2026-10-11, M-1 and L-4). It begins when the installation starts the service (the MSI starts it, `ServiceControl Start='install'`, and the package runs `NET START MySQL`) and ends with the `ALTER USER` statement of the hardening. It covers the rest of the Chocolatey installation, locating the installation and disabling the firewall exception, the permission checks (with the service stopped), and, after the restart, the pipe check and the wait for other connections. Its length was not measured. During the phase, `root@localhost` has no password and the server listens only on its named pipe; the server treats pipe connections as `localhost`. Because of the pipe's DACL (Everyone read and write, low integrity label), any local process can connect; because `PIPE_REJECT_REMOTE_CLIENTS` is not set, users of other computers who can authenticate to this computer over SMB (port 445, File and Printer Sharing) can connect as well. The installation guide therefore asks to check the firewall for port 445 and to run the script only when no untrusted user or program is active.
  * Checks against misuse of the phase: (a) the server holds the pipe exclusively only while its process runs (`FILE_FLAG_FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE`, no `FILE_CREATE_PIPE_INSTANCE` for others); a client that is `root` during the phase could shut the server down and create a pipe with the same name. The script therefore checks that the pipe's server process is the service immediately before it connects (`GetNamedPipeServerProcessId`; the pipe is opened with `SECURITY_SQOS_PRESENT` and `SECURITY_IDENTIFICATION`, so that its server cannot impersonate the elevated script) and that the service process is unchanged after the account creation. Microsoft Learn describes the handle for `GetNamedPipeServerProcessId` as one "created by the CreateNamedPipe function"; that it returns the server process for a client handle was verified by the helper tests on the development machine (Windows 11, PowerShell 5.1 and 7) with a pipe served by a child process (the child's process ID is returned, not the test's). (b) The script waits until no other client is connected, and the first statement of the hardening aborts the batch if another client is connected (all threads except its own and background threads with COMMAND `Daemon`); the same check runs again after the hardening. (c) After the hardening and before the application database is created (`CREATE DATABASE` without `IF NOT EXISTS`, so that a database created by someone else makes it fail): only the reserved accounts exist, none without a password, every account except `mariadb.sys` and `PUBLIC` uses `mysql_native_password` without an authentication chain (`$.plugin`, no `$.auth_or` in `mysql.global_priv.Priv`, the format `mariadb-install-db.exe` writes), the three `root` accounts have the same password hash, `mariadb.sys` is locked, no account except `root` has global privileges; only the schemas `information_schema`, `mysql`, `performance_schema` and `sys` exist; there are no events and no triggers in any schema (security re-check of 2026-10-12, N-1: the bootstrap creates none, and an event in `mysql` could otherwise run between the checks and the restart); `@@event_scheduler` is `OFF` (documented default) or `DISABLED`; every routine and view in any schema is one of the 153 bootstrap objects of MariaDB 12.3.3 (`mysql.user` and the `sys` schema, extracted from the sources at the tag `mariadb-12.3.3`) with the definer `mariadb.sys@localhost`; `mysql.db`, `columns_priv`, `procs_priv`, `roles_mapping`, `plugin`, `func` and `servers` are empty, `tables_priv` and `proxies_priv` contain only the bootstrap rows of the kept accounts; the general log is off, and shared memory is off if the server has the variable at all (not present in 12.3; read from `information_schema.GLOBAL_VARIABLES`). (d) The restart before the switch to TCP resets every `SET GLOBAL` change, and `my.ini` sets `event-scheduler=DISABLED` (cannot be changed at runtime). After the restart, the server variables of the listener, the logs and the event scheduler are checked, and all checks of (c) run again over TCP as `root@127.0.0.1` (which also tests that login), now with the application account (no global privileges, `SHOW GRANTS` exactly the application privileges on the two databases, the `mysql.db` rows checked). `root@localhost` is tested over the pipe, `root@127.0.0.1` over TCP; `root@::1` cannot be tested because the server listens only on `127.0.0.1`, and is covered by the identical password hash: equal hashes mean the same password (the MariaDB documentation of `PASSWORD()`, which `ALTER USER ... IDENTIFIED BY` uses, shows one fixed hash for a given password; whether the hash is unsalted is not stated, and a salted hash would make the check fail, not pass). Not covered: files that a client could have written with the rights of the service account during the phase (e.g. `SELECT ... INTO OUTFILE` into folders the service account can write, such as the data directory), and changed bodies of the bootstrap routines and views (only names, types and definers are compared). The allowlist matches MariaDB 12.3.3; another version may fail the check and needs the list updated. This residual risk is accepted for a development machine; a stage or production installation is not done with this script.
  * Interrupted or repeated runs (security review of 2026-10-11, L-2): after an interruption, the script waits for Windows Installer to finish before it stops and disables the service; on a new run, a MariaDB service whose setup did not complete (start type not `Automatic`, or `my.ini` still with networking skipped or the named pipe on) is stopped and disabled before anything else.
  * If `SKIPNETWORKING` does not take effect, the server listens on TCP (on all interfaces, MariaDB's default `bind_address`) until the script stops at the check of `my.ini`, and disables the service. During that time `root` has no password for `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1` and the computer's host name; a connection from another computer matches `root@<host name>` only if the server resolves the client's address to this host name (not expected, not verified). The installation deletes the anonymous accounts.
  * After the setup, the named pipe is disabled, shared memory is off or not available (not present in 12.3), and the server listens only on `127.0.0.1:3306`; verified with `Get-NetTCPConnection` and inside the server (`@@bind_address`, `@@skip_networking`, `@@named_pipe`, `@@port`, `SHARED_MEMORY` in `information_schema.GLOBAL_VARIABLES` `OFF` or absent, log settings, feedback plugin not active). The MSI's inbound firewall exception for `mysqld.exe` is disabled.
  * The service runs under its virtual account instead of `LocalSystem` (D-027 noted `LocalSystem` as residual risk for MySQL).
