Program folder and `bin` folder lie below `%ProgramFiles%`, which ordinary users cannot change; the script fails if any item there grants write access to other accounts than Administrators, SYSTEM, TrustedInstaller and CREATOR OWNER, or is a reparse point. The data directory is restricted to Administrators, SYSTEM and the service account (protected DACL; explicit entries of its children removed); every item is checked afterwards.
  * The MSI's verbose log (`%TEMP%\<package>.<version>.MsiInstall.log`) contains the MSI properties; no password is passed. The temporary option file with a password is created with its security descriptor set at creation (Administrators and SYSTEM only, owner Administrators; security review of 2026-10-11, L-1).
  * Accepted (project owner, 2026-10-12; security review of 2026-10-12, L-1): the script does not check that a version given with `-MariaDbVersion` matches the version of the installed server. The script is used only for local development machines; stage and production servers are set up differently (expected to run Linux, not decided, D-008).
* Not verified (to be confirmed by the first real run of the setup script):
  * that `msiexec` accepts `SKIPNETWORKING=1` after the package's arguments (the script fails closed if `my.ini` lacks `skip-networking`);
  * that the package's `NET START MySQL` succeeds although the MSI starts the service itself (`ServiceControl Start='install'`);
  * the actual owners and DACLs of the program folder and the data directory, the exact content of `my.ini`, the name of the server executable (`mysqld.exe` or `mariadbd.exe`), whether `PUBLIC` appears in `mysql.global_priv`, and that `ALTER USER IF EXISTS` with three accounts succeeds;
  * that root can log in over TCP from `127.0.0.1` with the new password, and that Django's test suite runs with the application account's privileges (now without `REFERENCES`);
  * that `msiexec /x <ProductCode>` removes the service and the data directory (MSI `CLEANUPDATA` default 1);
  * that the shared memory checks via `information_schema.GLOBAL_VARIABLES` pass on MariaDB 12.3.3 (no `SHARED_MEMORY` row expected) and that the server starts with the switched `my.ini` without a `shared-memory` line (next run of the setup script);
  * the CI step with the application account and the new database tests (tag `mariadb`) have not run yet; the earlier CI run with `root` passed both jobs including the database tests, which also showed that the health check works with the official images;
  * the recommendation that MariaDB 12.3 needs fewer resources than MySQL for this application.
* Alternatives: keep MySQL (decided against by the project owner); MariaDB 11.8 or 11.4 (older long-term support releases; D-015 asks for the newest); passing the root password to the MSI (rejected: command line and Chocolatey log); keeping the named pipe enabled after the setup (rejected: reachable by every local process and not restricted to local clients; Django uses TCP).
* Sources: as listed in the verified facts; https://mariadb.org/about/, https://downloads.mariadb.org/rest-api/mariadb/, https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.0/ref/databases/, https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.1/ref/databases/, https://mariadb.com/docs/server/reference/data-types/string-data-types/character-sets/supported-character-sets-and-collations, https://jira.mariadb.org/browse/MDEV-35256, https://mariadb.com/docs/server/server-management/variables-and-modes/server-system-variables, https://hub.docker.com/_/mariadb, https://mariadb.com/docs/server/server-management/automated-mariadb-deployment-and-administration/docker-and-mariadb/using-healthcheck-sh, https://docs.chocolatey.org/en-us/choco/commands/install/, https://community.chocolatey.org/packages/mariadb.install, https://mariadb.com/docs/server/server-management/install-and-upgrade-mariadb/installing-mariadb/binary-packages/installing-mariadb-msi-packages-on-windows, https://mariadb.com/kb/en/mysql_install_dbexe/, https://github.com/MariaDB/server (branch 12.3), https://mariadb.com/docs/server/server-management/install-and-upgrade-mariadb/configuring-mariadb/configuring-mariadb-with-option-files, https://mariadb.com/docs/server/clients-and-utilities/mariadb-client/mariadb-command-line-client, https://mariadb.com/docs/server/server-management/install-and-upgrade-mariadb/configuring-mariadb/mariadb-environment-variables, https://github.com/mariadb-corporation/mariadb-connector-c, https://mariadb.com/docs/server/reference/sql-statements/account-management-sql-statements/grant, https://jira.mariadb.org/browse/MDEV-39047, https://mariadb.com/docs/server/mariadb-quickstart-guides/mariadb-indexes-guide, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winbase/nf-winbase-getnamedpipeserverprocessid, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/fileapi/nf-fileapi-createfilew, https://github.com/dotnet/runtime (`src/libraries/Common/src/Interop/Windows/Kernel32/Interop.SecurityOptions.cs`) (all checked on 2026-10-11)
* Consequences: all statements about MySQL in the documentation are updated or marked as history; `docs/INSTALLATION.md` describes the MariaDB setup; `docs/THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` notes MariaDB Server.

## D-031: Collations

* Date: 2026-10-11
* Status: Decided (project owner, 2026-10-12; proposed after the security review of 2026-10-11, M-2). Applies when the custom user model and the token tables are implemented (D-021).
* Context: The databases use the character set `utf8mb4` with the collation `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` (D-028, D-030), which is case- and accent-insensitive ("_ai" accent insensitive, "_ci" case insensitive). Comparisons in such a collation treat different strings as equal, e.g. `josé@example.org` and `jose@example.org`. For identifiers this would make a unique index reject a different address and a lookup find another account.
* Decision:
  * Columns that hold identifiers and are compared exactly use the binary collation `utf8mb4_bin` through Django's `db_collation` field option: the normalised email address of the uniqueness column (D-016), token and keyed-hash values (D-023), and the customer number (D-021). Normalisation (D-021: NFKC, surrounding white space removed, lower case, IDN domain mapped to ASCII) stays in the application.
  * All other text columns keep the database default `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci`.
  * Every database of an instance is created with `CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` (the local setup script does so; the installation guide states it for operators); Django's test database receives the same through `TEST["CHARSET"]` and `TEST["COLLATION"]`.
* Verified facts:
  * Django: `CharField.db_collation` and `TextField.db_collation`: "Optional. The database collation name of the field." "Collation names are not standardized." (Django documentation, "Model field reference", 6.0).
  * MariaDB: `utf8mb4_bin` (id 46) and `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` are listed in "Supported Character Sets and Collations" (list "from MariaDB 11.4.5"); the UCA-14.0.0 collations exist from 10.10.1. For 10.11, the existence of both is proven by a database test that applies both collations (`COLLATE`), run in CI against 10.11 and 12.3.
  * Defaults: `character_set_server` is `latin1` up to MariaDB 11.5 and `utf8mb4` from 11.6; the default collation of `utf8mb4` is `utf8mb4_general_ci` up to 11.4 and `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` from 11.5 (MariaDB documentation, "Server System Variables" and "Supported Character Sets and Collations"). A database created on MariaDB 10.11 without explicit options would therefore be `latin1`; the project always sets both explicitly. A database test checks the default character set and collation of the (test) database.
  * `utf8mb4_bin` uses PAD SPACE (trailing spaces are ignored in comparisons; `utf8mb4_nopad_bin` would not ignore them). The values of these columns never end with spaces (normalised addresses, URL-safe tokens and hashes, digits), so PAD SPACE does not matter for them.
* Alternatives: an accent- and case-sensitive UCA collation (rejected: still linguistic, not byte-exact); `utf8mb4_nopad_bin` (equivalent for these values; `utf8mb4_bin` chosen as the established binary collation named in the review).
* Sources: https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.0/ref/models/fields/, https://mariadb.com/docs/server/reference/data-types/string-data-types/character-sets/supported-character-sets-and-collations, https://mariadb.com/docs/server/server-management/variables-and-modes/server-system-variables (checked on 2026-10-11)
* Consequences: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` ("User Model and Login Identifiers") and `docs/INSTALLATION.md` describe the collations; tests of the user model must cover addresses that differ only in accents or case.

## D-032: Profile Data Structure Based on eCH-0010

* Date: 2026-10-12
* Status: Decided (project owner, 2026-10-12). Applies when the custom user model is implemented (D-021). Amended by D-036 (2026-10-12): the structure stays; unused fields are hidden, the shown fields have everyday labels and forms show one postal code field.
* Context: The profile needs the name and postal address for invoices (`docs/PROJECT.md`, "Profile Data and Purpose"). Accounts may represent a private person or an organisation (e.g. a property management). Unmarried couples with different names want both names on the invoice. Owners or property managements may live abroad.
* Decision:
  * The profile fields follow the person, organisation and address structure of eCH-0010 (postal address). All fields of these parts are created, even if some are rarely used (project owner: better to have them than to need a migration later).
  * Person: salutation (optional; "Mrs", "Mr", "Family" or empty), title (optional), first name, last name.
  * Organisation: organisation name and two name add-ons (optional).
  * Either the organisation name or first and last name are required. With an organisation name, the person's salutation, title, first and last name are optional (contact person).
  * Address: two free address lines (optional, e.g. a second person on the invoice such as "and Beat Example", or "c/o"), street, house number (text, e.g. "12a"), dwelling number, post office box number and text, locality, town, postal code, country (default Switzerland). Swiss postal codes have four digits (1000-9999); foreign postal codes are text of up to 15 characters.
  * Required address fields: postal code, town, country and either a street or a post office box; the house number is optional, so that addresses without a house number and post office box addresses are possible (project owner, 2026-10-12; eCH-0010 itself makes only the town and the country mandatory).
  * The postal address uses the town ("Ort") of the postal address, not the political municipality (see D-013).
  * One account belongs to exactly one person or organisation; there are no shared accounts. A second person is named in an address line, not in the name fields.
* Verified facts (eCH-0010 XML schema `eCH-0010-8-0.xsd`, published with version 8.1.0, approved 14 July 2025):
  * `personMailAddressInfoType`: `mrMrs` (optional, values 1 and 2 only), `title` (optional, max. 50), `firstName` (optional, max. 30), `lastName` (required, max. 30).
  * `organisationMailAddressInfoType`: `organisationName` (required, max. 60), `organisationNameAddOn1` and `organisationNameAddOn2` (optional), `mrMrs`, `title`, `firstName`, `lastName` (all optional).
  * `addressInformationType`: `addressLine1`, `addressLine2` (optional, max. 150), `street` (max. 150), `houseNumber` (max. 30), `dwellingNumber` (max. 30), `postOfficeBoxNumber`, `postOfficeBoxText` (max. 15), `locality` (max. 40), `town` (required, max. 40), `swissZipCode` (1000-9999) with optional add-on and ID, or `foreignZipCode` (max. 15), `country` (ISO 3166 alpha-2 code and short name).
* Deviations from eCH-0010: the salutation "Family" does not exist in `mrMrs`; it is lost in a future eCH export. Whether eCH-0010 intends the address lines for a second person was not verified (the main document could not be read).
* Security and data protection: all additional fields are optional and serve the stated purpose (postal address for invoices; DSG Art. 6 para. 3). Field lengths are limited; the eCH lengths are the reference.
* Alternatives: two accounts for a couple (rejected: both names would still not appear on one invoice); both names in the first and last name fields (rejected: breaks sorting and search by name); a shared account (rejected: shared credentials, no traceability).
* Sources: https://www.ech.ch/de/ech/ech-0010 (version 8.1.0), https://www.ech.ch/sites/default/files/imce/eCH-Dossier/0001-0030/eCH-0010/8.1.0/Beilagen/eCH-0010-8-0.xsd (checked on 2026-10-12)
* Consequences: `docs/PROJECT.md` ("Profile Data and Purpose") lists the fields; the privacy notice of the instance must name the purposes.

## D-033: Address Validation and Autocompletion with the Official Directory of Building Addresses

* Date: 2026-10-12
* Status: Decided in principle (project owner, 2026-10-12); details open (see below).
* Context: The project owner wants entered addresses to be validated and an autocompletion to be offered, as part of phase 1 (creating, updating and deleting the profile are fully covered in phase 1).
* Decision:
  * The official directory of building addresses (swisstopo) is downloaded regularly and stored in an optimised form in a separate database (project owner, 2026-10-12). It is public data and is not part of the application's backups, so backups stay small; stage and production (and several instances) on the same server may use the same address database.
  * Each environment and instance accesses the address database with its own account that may only read (`SELECT`). Only a separate import account may write. The application has no foreign keys into the address database; the profile stores the address as entered.
  * Swiss addresses are checked against it during registration and profile editing, and an autocompletion is offered. The check warns but does not block: foreign addresses and very new buildings are not in the directory.
  * Entered addresses are never sent to an external service; the autocompletion queries only the local copy.
* Verified facts (swisstopo data catalogue, data.geo.admin.ch, STAC API, checked on 2026-10-12):
  * "Official directory of building addresses" (ID 197 of the catalogue of basic geodata): contains all official building addresses of Switzerland and Liechtenstein and is binding for public authorities.
  * Assets per country as CSV (ZIP), FileGDB and INTERLIS, each with a SHA-256 multihash checksum; the Swiss data set was last updated on 2026-10-11.
  * The licence link refers to the swisstopo terms of use for open government data (https://www.swisstopo.admin.ch/ogd-conditions).
* Open and not yet verified:
  * the content of the terms of use (e.g. attribution);
  * the columns of the CSV file (whether postal code and town are included per address) and the update interval;
  * how the import is scheduled per server and how its integrity is verified (checksum from the catalogue over HTTPS);
  * the Django configuration: a second database alias with a database router, migrations for that alias, and a test database for it (the local setup script and continuous integration must provide it);
  * rate limiting of the autocompletion endpoint, which is reachable without login during registration.
* Security implications: the directory contains no personal data. Sharing the address database deliberately crosses the separation of stage and production (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, "Environments"): a compromised stage could read it but not change it, because every environment has its own read-only account and separate credentials (`docs/SECURITY.md`, section 7); a compromised import account could change addresses shown to all environments, which affects only suggestions and warnings, never stored profiles. The import is an outbound HTTPS download from a federal service; the downloaded file is verified before use. The autocompletion endpoint must be rate-limited and must not reveal anything about accounts.
* Sources: https://data.geo.admin.ch/api/stac/v0.9/collections/ch.swisstopo.amtliches-gebaeudeadressverzeichnis and `/items` (checked on 2026-10-12)
* Consequences: `docs/PROJECT.md` lists the function in phase 1.

## D-034: Data Model of the Custom User Model

* Date: 2026-10-12
* Status: Decided (project owner, 2026-10-12: data model only; profile fields directly on the user model; countries with `django-countries`, default Switzerland; optional phone number, validated and stored in E.164 with `django-phonenumber-field` and `phonenumberslite`). The other points implement D-016, D-021, D-031 and D-032 and are recorded for traceability.
* Context: README "Current Status", next step 3 (D-021). The custom user model must exist before any persistent database is migrated.
* Decision:
  * Scope of this step: the app `leg_platform.accounts` with the models, the first migration and tests. No views, forms, URLs, templates, admin registration of the user model, login backend, `django-axes` or `django-otp` wiring.
  * The profile fields of D-032 are fields of the user model `accounts.User`; there is no separate profile table. Lengths follow eCH-0010. The Swiss postal code is an integer from 1000 to 9999, the post office box number an integer of at most 8 digits. Rules that depend on several fields (organisation name or first and last name; street or post office box; post office box text for a post office box number; town and country required; Swiss postal code for Switzerland, foreign postal code otherwise) are checked in `User.clean()`, which Django's model forms call. Amended by D-036: no rule requires a hidden field (no post office box text for a post office box number); the postal code rules are checked in `User.clean_fields()`.
  * Country: `CountryField` of `django-countries` (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code), default `CH`; country names come from the package's translations.
  * Phone number: optional `PhoneNumberField` of `django-phonenumber-field`; `PHONENUMBER_DEFAULT_REGION = "CH"` (numbers without international prefix are read as Swiss numbers) and `PHONENUMBER_DB_FORMAT = "E164"` (the package default, set explicitly). `phonenumberslite` is declared directly instead of the extra `django-phonenumber-field[phonenumberslite]`, so that the minimum CI job pins it; the lite variant omits the geocoder, carrier and time zone metadata, which are not needed.
  * Customer numbers (D-021): the table `IssuedCustomerNumber` holds every number ever issued, as primary key, without reference to a person. A number is drawn with `secrets.randbelow(10**6)` and inserted in a savepoint; a collision is retried, at most 100 times; when all numbers are issued, issuing fails without drawing. Rows of this table cannot be changed or deleted through the model or its queryset; `save()` always inserts (`force_insert`), so an existing number is never overwritten, and `bulk_create()` is rejected. `User.customer_number` is a one-to-one foreign key to this table (`on_delete=PROTECT`, column `customer_number`), so the database accepts only issued numbers; `customer_number_id` holds the six-digit string and `customer_number_display` the display format. A foreign key column takes the type and collation of the referenced column (Django source `ForeignKey.db_parameters()`, installed Django 6.1.2), here `utf8mb4_bin`; a test reads `information_schema.COLUMNS` to confirm it (security review finding L-3). The number is set only by `assign_customer_number()`, for a saved, active account without a number (L-5).
  * String representation (project owner, 2026-10-12, Q-3, clarified after re-review): `User.__str__()` never shows an email address or customer number; it is "Account 17" using the internal primary key (Swiss German "Konto 17"). The ID provides a way to find the corresponding user during support; the later admin must expose the internal ID in the user list. Changed by D-036 (2026-10-12): the account display and the admin user list use the customer number; admin log entries keep only the internal number (D-018), which the change form shows as "internal number".
  * Deleting an account deletes its admin log entries (`LogEntry.user` cascades); accepted by the project owner on 2026-10-12 (Q-4).
  * Compiled translations (`.mo`) are committed next to their `.po` files; CI compiles each `.po` with GNU `msgfmt --check` and compares the decompiled result with the committed `.mo` (`msgunfmt`). Developers regenerate them with `python manage.py compilemessages`, which needs GNU gettext.
  * Login identifier (D-016, D-021, D-031): `normalized_email` is the `USERNAME_FIELD`: unique, nullable, not editable, collation `utf8mb4_bin`. `email` holds the canonical address (D-035) and `normalized_email` is derived from it in `save()` with `normalize_email_address()`; it is `NULL` while the account is deactivated. Validation rejects non-ASCII local parts (D-035), quoted local parts, domain literals, IP addresses, `localhost` and characters of the Unicode categories C, Z and M; it validates the canonical address with Django's `EmailValidator`. `idna` is a direct dependency so Unicode and Punycode domain spellings use the same unique key. Whether mail providers deliver all mapped variants to the same mailbox has not been verified.
  * Invariants enforced by the model, independent of any view or admin: `is_active` is derived from the status (only active accounts are active); unverified accounts have neither a customer number nor a usable password; `create_user()` creates an unverified account without usable password and rejects a password and the fields only the account logic sets (status, privileges, groups, permissions, customer number, retention flag); `create_superuser()` refuses while any account with `is_superuser` exists (`docs/PROJECT.md`, "First System Administrator"); a customer number never changes; the retention flag can only change from unset to set, checked against the stored row read with `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`; `QuerySet.update()` of protected fields (status, `is_active`, email, normalised email, retention flag, `is_staff`, `is_superuser`, customer number, password), `QuerySet.delete()` and `bulk_create()` are rejected (`bulk_update()` uses `update()`; related managers such as `group.user_set` use the same queryset). The protecting queryset is also the base manager (`Meta.base_manager_name = "objects"`), so that `_base_manager` does not bypass it.
  * Central account logic (Q-2): status, email address (with the normalised column), `is_active` (derived), `is_staff`, `is_superuser`, customer number and retention flag change only through `set_status()`, `change_email()`, `set_privileges()`, `assign_customer_number()` and `set_retention_obligation()`. Every `save()` of an existing account compares these fields with the row locked with `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` and rejects changes that were not made through these methods, which also rejects a full `save()` of a stale instance. `update_fields` that contain one of email, status, normalised email or `is_active` are extended to all four (M-1). New accounts are always inserted (`force_insert`), the primary key of a loaded account cannot change, and `create_user()` rejects `id`/`pk` and the controlled fields (M-2). `delete()` refuses accounts with a retention obligation and the last active system administrator (M-4).
  * `create_superuser()` requires a password, runs Django's password validators, refuses inside a transaction and serialises concurrent calls with a MariaDB named lock (`GET_LOCK`), because no row exists yet that could be locked; a concurrency test covers it. Django's `createsuperuser` command is replaced by one that refuses (D-005) (M-5).
* Verified facts:
  * Django 6.1 documentation, "Managers": Django uses `Model._base_manager` "when accessing related objects"; "If the normal base manager class (django.db.models.Manager) isn't appropriate for your circumstances, you can tell Django which class to use by setting Meta.base_manager_name"; a base manager should not "override get_queryset() to filter out any rows". The queryset filters nothing; tests cover related-object access (`LogEntry.user`), lookups across the relation and cascading deletion.
  * `django-phonenumber-field` documentation ("Settings"): `PHONENUMBER_DB_FORMAT` default `"E164"`; `PHONENUMBER_DEFAULT_REGION` default `None`; "When no region is specified, a phone number in the "NATIONAL" format will be assumed to be from the PHONENUMBER_DEFAULT_REGION". The installed source of 8.5.0 reads both settings with these defaults.
  * eCH-0010 XML schema `eCH-0010-8-0.xsd` (checked again on 2026-10-12): `postOfficeBoxNumberType` is `xs:unsignedInt` with `maxInclusive` 99999999; all other lengths as listed in D-032.
  * Packages (PyPI JSON API, 2026-10-12):

    | Package | Minimum / current release | Licence | Python | Django | Dependencies |
    | ------- | ------------------------ | ------- | ------ | ------ | ------------ |
    | `django-countries` | 9.1.0 (2026-09-10) | MIT | ≥ 3.10; classifiers 3.10 to 3.14 | classifiers 4.2, 5.2, 6.0, 6.1 | `asgiref`, `typing-extensions` |
    | `django-phonenumber-field` | 8.5.0 (2026-07-23) | MIT | ≥ 3.10; classifiers 3.10 to 3.14 | `Django>=4.2`; classifiers 4.2, 5.2, 6.0 | extras only |
    | `phonenumberslite` | 9.0.41 (2026-10-08) | Apache-2.0 | ≥ 2.5; classifiers up to 3.12 | – | none |
    | `idna` | 3.15 / 3.20 (2026-05-12 / 2026-09-17) | BSD-3-Clause | ≥ 3.8 / ≥ 3.9 | – | none |

  * PyPI reports no vulnerabilities for these releases (`vulnerabilities` field empty); the GitHub Advisory Database lists no advisories for the selected `django-countries`, `django-phonenumber-field`, `phonenumberslite` and `idna` releases (checked on 2026-10-12). The IDNA advisory CVE-2026-45409 affects versions below 3.15; the selected idna 3.20 is above the patched version (GHSA-65pc-fj4g-8rjx).
  * The German catalogue of `django-countries` 9.1.0 contains "ß" in one entry, the official name "United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland"; the displayed name is the common name ("Vereinigtes Königreich"). The project catalogue overrides the official name. The German catalogue of `django-phonenumber-field` 8.5.0 contains no "ß". Tests check both catalogues and Django's German texts for the account fields against the project catalogue.
  * Local verification update (2026-10-12, Python 3.14.8, Django 6.1.2, MariaDB 12.3.3): system checks and migration checks pass; the full suite has one failure because the reserved-domain repository check finds six non-reserved email fixtures in the normalization tests. The new admin tests pass.
  * Translation verification (2026-10-12): `compilemessages`, `msgfmt --check`, comparison via `msgunfmt` against a fresh compilation, and runtime loading of the new Swiss German admin messages all pass. The CI workflow itself has not run.
* Not verified:
  * the OSV database (not queried);
  * whether releases older than the chosen minimums are free of known vulnerabilities (as in D-026, the minimum is the current release);
  * `phonenumberslite` on Python 3.13 and 3.14 and `django-phonenumber-field` on Django 6.1 are not declared in their classifiers; compatibility rests on the tests (locally Python 3.14 with Django 6.1; the CI jobs with these packages have not run yet);
  * the completed user interface translations have not been verified in page rendering because the phase-1 application views are not implemented yet;
  * the CI check of compiled translations (it has not run yet);
* Security implications: the invariants are enforced in the application. Code can still bypass them with raw SQL or Django's private `QuerySet._update()`, and persons with database access can change rows directly; database privileges are restricted (`docs/SECURITY.md`, section 9). Fixtures loaded with `loaddata` are saved raw (`save_base(raw=True)`) and bypass `save()` and its checks; group and permission changes through many-to-many managers (`user.groups.add()`, `group.user_set.add()`, `user.user_permissions.add()`) are not checked by the model (security review of 2026-10-12, L-8). The admin restricts group, permission and privilege changes to system administrators (`docs/SECURITY.md`, section 4); account creation and deletion are not available there. No fixtures with accounts are used. The phone number is optional personal data for queries (D-016). The customer number generator uses `secrets`; the number is not a secret (D-021).
* Alternatives: a separate profile model (rejected by the project owner); `phonenumbers` instead of `phonenumberslite` (rejected: unused metadata); own validation of phone numbers or an own country list (rejected: established packages are preferred, `AGENTS.md`, section 7); database constraints for the cross-field rules (not chosen: the rules are form-level requirements of a complete profile and are checked in `clean()`).
* Sources: https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.1/topics/db/managers/, https://www.gnu.org/software/gettext/manual/html_node/msgfmt-Invocation.html and https://www.gnu.org/software/gettext/manual/html_node/msgunfmt-Invocation.html (checked on 2026-10-12), installed Django source `django/db/models/fields/related.py` (`ForeignKey.db_parameters`), https://django-phonenumber-field.readthedocs.io/en/latest/reference.html, https://www.ech.ch/sites/default/files/imce/eCH-Dossier/0001-0030/eCH-0010/8.1.0/Beilagen/eCH-0010-8-0.xsd, https://pypi.org/pypi/django-countries/json, https://pypi.org/pypi/django-phonenumber-field/json, https://pypi.org/pypi/phonenumberslite/json, https://pypi.org/pypi/phonenumberslite/9.0.41/json, https://pypi.org/pypi/idna/3.20/json, https://github.com/advisories (all checked on 2026-10-12); https://github.com/kjd/idna (UTS #46 API and supported Python versions; checked on 2026-10-12); https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-65pc-fj4g-8rjx (CVE-2026-45409, checked on 2026-10-12); installed sources and catalogues of the profile packages
* Consequences: `pyproject.toml`, `requirements/minimum-constraints.txt` and `docs/THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` list the packages. Registration, the login backend, `django-axes` and `django-otp` follow in later steps.

## D-035: Canonical Form of Account Email Addresses

- Date: 2026-10-12
- Status: Decided by the project owner (2026-10-12). Re-confirmed by the project owner on 2026-10-13 with knowledge of the risk statement below (lower case for the whole address, including the local part); no open owner decision remains.
- Context: The security re-review (findings L-2 and Q-1/N-1, see D-034) showed that the documents said accents in the local part are kept while the validator rejected them. The question was whether to store the entered address or a canonical form.
- Decision:
  - The local part of an account email address is ASCII only. Internationalised local parts (SMTPUTF8) are not accepted. Internationalised domains are accepted and stored as IDNA ASCII (D-034).
  - The canonical form is stored in `email`: Unicode NFKC, surrounding white space removed, lower case for the whole address, domain in IDNA ASCII (UTS #46). It is derived in `save()` (`_apply_derived_fields()`), which all account changes go through.
  - `normalized_email` holds the same value while the account is not deactivated, so both columns are equal for active accounts. It is `NULL` while deactivated (D-034).
  - Lookups, counters and rate limits use `normalize_email_address()` unchanged (D-021).
  - Local parts are stored in lower case and compared case-insensitively. This deviates from RFC 5321 section 2.4 (see Verified facts and Risks).
- Reason: One canonical value avoids two stored spellings of the same account. ASCII local parts avoid SMTPUTF8 transmission failures and accent look-alikes. The project owner's rationale: domains are not case-sensitive, and the owner assumes that the same name cannot be reserved in two cases, so all comparisons are done in lower case. This assumption is not verified (see Not verified).
- Verified facts:
  - RFC 6531 sections 3.2 and 3.5: non-ASCII local parts require SMTPUTF8. Section 3.4: SMTPUTF8 carries a transmission-failure risk. Section 3.2 and 3.7: IDNA domains can be used without SMTPUTF8.
  - RFC 5321 section 2.4 (fetched 2026-10-12): "The local-part of a mailbox MUST BE treated as case sensitive". Domains are not case sensitive. Lower-casing the local part therefore conflicts with this MUST.
- Not verified: Whether the mail services used by the municipalities treat local parts case-insensitively. Not tested against a real mail server.
- Risks:
  - Lower-casing the local part (RFC 5321 section 2.4) can send verification mail to a different mailbox on a host that distinguishes case, for example `Max@x` and `max@x`. The owner of that mailbox can then complete verification of a pending account or, once password reset exists, reset the password of an active account (account takeover). This needs a case-distinguishing host and two differently cased mailboxes; neither is verified. Because both spellings map to one account key, registering one spelling blocks the other (denial, not takeover). The security review of 2026-10-12 (finding H-1) found the first version of this risk text incorrect. The project owner must re-confirm the lower-casing decision with the corrected risk. Secure alternative (not chosen): keep the local part as entered, use the lower-case form only as lookup and uniqueness key, and send mail only to the stored address.
  - The stored address can differ from the entered one (Punycode domain, lower case). The user interface must show the stored form where it is shown.
- Alternatives: Keep the local part case-sensitive as RFC 5321 requires (not chosen by the project owner). Allow SMTPUTF8 for accented local parts (not chosen; see RFC 6531 section 3.4). Store the entered address and compare the canonical form only (not chosen; two stored spellings).
- Consequences: No schema change and no migration. Existing rows with a non-canonical `email` are not converted by this decision; check before any deployment with data. Tests and docs updated. The redundant `normalized_email` column remains; its removal would need a migration and is not decided.
- Sources: https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6531 (sections 3.2, 3.4, 3.5, 3.7); https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5321 (section 2.4), both checked 2026-10-12.

## D-036: Hidden eCH-0010 Fields and Everyday Wording

* Date: 2026-10-12
* Status: Decided (project owner, 2026-10-12). Amends D-032 (which profile fields are shown) and replaces the string-representation point of D-034 (Q-3). The migrations of `leg_platform.accounts` were regenerated as one initial migration and the development database was recreated (project owner, 2026-10-12; nothing is deployed, README "Current Status").
* Context: The admin showed technical terms taken from eCH-0010 and from Django ("Wohnungsnummer", "Ortschaft", "Postfachtext", "Namenszusatz 1 der Organisation", "Adresszeile 1", separate Swiss and foreign postal codes, "Django administration", "Mitarbeiter-Status", the internal ID). The project owner's binding rule: user-facing texts use generally established everyday terms, never invented technical terms.
* Decision:
  * The eCH-0010 structure stays unchanged in the database (D-032): all fields, names, types and lengths are kept, so a future eCH-0010 export needs no mapping beyond D-032.
  * eCH-0010 fields that are not used are kept but not shown in forms: title, organisation name add-ons 1 and 2, address line 2, dwelling number, post office box text and locality. Validation never requires a hidden field; a post office box number no longer needs a post office box text. Saving a form keeps the stored values of hidden fields. The phone number and the salutation "Family" stay.
  * Labels of the shown fields: `organisation_name` "company name" ("Firmenname"; messages "Enter a first name or a company name." / "Enter a last name or a company name."); `address_line_1` "address addition" ("Adresszusatz", help text "For example a second person or c/o.", e.g. a second person on the invoice; one account still belongs to exactly one person, D-032); `post_office_box_number` "post office box" ("Postfach").
  * Postal code: forms show one field "postal code" ("Postleitzahl"), which is not a model field. Its initial value is the stored Swiss or foreign postal code. On saving, it is written into `swiss_postal_code` for Switzerland (`CH`; four digits from 1000 to 9999, message "Enter a postal code with 4 digits from 1000 to 9999.") and into `foreign_postal_code` for every other country (required text of up to 15 characters); the other field is cleared. As before, only `CH` uses the Swiss postal code; Liechtenstein (`LI`) addresses use the foreign postal code. The model checks the same rules on the two fields in `User.clean_fields()`, which skips fields a form excludes, as Django's documentation recommends ("How to raise field-specific validation errors if those fields don't appear in a ModelForm").
  * Account display (`User.__str__`, used in the admin list, page titles and error output): "Account 686 821" with the customer number in its display format (Swiss German "Konto 686 821"); "Unconfirmed account" ("Nicht bestätigtes Konto") without a customer number; never the email address or the name. It reads `customer_number_id` from the row and makes no query.
  * Admin log entries stay neutral (D-018; project owner, 2026-10-12): for accounts, `LogEntry.object_repr` stores "Account with internal number 17" in the default language of the instance ("Konto mit interner Nummer 17"), never the customer number (it appears on invoices), the email address or the name. `UserAdmin` overrides all logging hooks of `ModelAdmin` (`log_addition`, `log_change`, `log_deletions`) instead of `LogEntry.objects.log_actions()`, which would store `str(obj)`. `LogEntry.object_id` holds the same internal number. The change form shows it as "internal number" ("Interne Nummer"), so a system administrator can map a log entry to the account while the account exists. After the account is deleted, the mapping is intentionally lost: the entry keeps only the internal number. It refers to no person only as long as no other kept record maps that number to a person (e.g. a backup, a future security log or invoices that reference the account's internal number); such records must be considered when they are introduced. The actor of an entry (`LogEntry.user`) is a foreign key; the history page reads the actor's email address (the login identifier; the user model has no `get_full_name()`) from the account when it is displayed, nothing of it is stored in the entry. Future admin code that logs an account as a related or inline object (e.g. an inline on another model) must keep the stored text neutral as well; reviews check this, and a test must cover it when such code is added.
  * History of accounts: only system administrators can open it (project owner, 2026-10-12; security review finding N-1), because it shows the email address of the acting account. `UserAdmin.history_view()` answers other users with 403, as the other system-administrator-only admin pages do (the Group admin), before looking up the account, so the response does not reveal whether it exists; the change form shows the "History" link only to system administrators (template `admin/accounts/user/change_form_object_tools.html`). The Group admin, including its history, is already restricted to system administrators. "Recent actions" on the admin index lists only the viewer's own actions (Django's `get_admin_log ... for_user user`) and shows the stored neutral text, no actor.
  * History texts: Django stores the English form label of each changed field (e.g. "First name") and translates it only when the history is shown (`LogEntry.get_change_message()`, installed Django 6.1.2 source `django/contrib/admin/models.py`; labels from `construct_change_message()` in `django/contrib/admin/utils.py`). The project catalogue therefore contains the capitalised labels of all editable account and group fields, so the history reads e.g. "Vorname geändert."; a test checks every editable label.
  * The admin user list shows the customer number ("Kundennummer", display format) instead of the internal ID and sorts by it. The search matches parts of the stored digits: "686821" finds that number; "686 821" is split into two terms and finds every account whose number contains both "686" and "821", not only an exact match. The change form shows the customer number and the internal number.
  * The user model is called "account"/"accounts" ("Konto"/"Konten").
  * `is_staff` is labelled "access to administration" ("Zugang zur Verwaltung"); the admin form messages use the same words ("A system administrator also needs access to the administration.", "Only active accounts can receive access to the administration."). Django's texts of `is_superuser`, `groups` and `user_permissions` are overridden in the project catalogue so that they speak of the system administrator ("Systemadministrator") and the account instead of "Administrator-Status" and "Benutzer".
  * Admin titles: "LEG Platform" ("LEG-Plattform") as site header and title, "Administration" ("Verwaltung") as index title; no "Django". Instance-specific names follow with the instance configuration (README, "Current Status").
  * Duplicate email address: "This email address is already in use." ("Diese E-Mail-Adresse wird bereits verwendet.") as the unique message of the login column, intended for the later email change in the account's own profile; it does not appear in the admin, where the email address cannot be changed. Public registration must not show it and must answer duplicates generically (account enumeration, security review finding L-9; `docs/SECURITY.md`, section 3). Django's generic unique message is overridden with "%(model_name)s: Dieser Wert für «%(field_label)s» wird bereits verwendet." (e.g. "Gruppe: Dieser Wert für «Name» wird bereits verwendet."), which fits every field; the translation keeps both placeholders of the source text, because GNU `msgfmt --check` rejects a translation that omits one (CI run 38294037420).
  * Texts of Django 6.1 without a German translation in Django that appear on the admin pages ("- Select an option -", "Run", and the screen-reader headings "Search %(name)s" and "Pagination %(name)s") are translated in the project catalogue.
* Deviations from eCH-0010: none in the data structure beyond D-032 (the salutation "Family" has no `mrMrs` value). Note for a future export: eCH-0010 section 2.1 states that post office box addresses need a post office box text and optionally a number ("Postfachadressen benötigen einen Postfachtext und optional eine Postfachnummer"), and section 2.18 that the text is given in the desired language. Because the post office box text is hidden, an export must fill it (e.g. "Postfach") whenever a post office box number is stored.
* Liechtenstein postal codes (checked on 2026-10-12; current behaviour kept): Liechtenstein addresses use four-digit postal codes of the Swiss system (Swiss Post: its postal code data covers "die vier- und sechsstelligen Postleitzahlen der Schweiz und des Fürstentums Liechtenstein", https://www.post.ch/de/geschaeftlich/themen-a-z/adressen-pflegen-und-geodaten-nutzen/adress-und-geodaten; swisstopo: the official directory of localities with postal codes covers "aller Ortschaften der Schweiz und des Fürstentums Liechtenstein" and is updated with reports of the Swiss Post, https://www.swisstopo.admin.ch/de/amtliches-ortschaftenverzeichnis). eCH-0010 8.1.0 does not mention Liechtenstein: it defines `swissZipCode` as "Von der Schweizer Post vergebene Postleitzahl" (section 2.19) and `foreignZipCode` as "Von einer Post im Ausland vergebene Postleitzahl" (section 2.22), and the XSD has no annotation on either element (https://www.ech.ch/de/ech/ech-0010, main document and `eCH-0010-8-0.xsd`). No official source found states which element applies to Liechtenstein or who assigns its postal codes. Liechtenstein therefore keeps the foreign postal code (text) until eCH or the project owner clarifies it; no range is enforced.
* Verified facts:
  * Django 6.1 documentation, `Model.clean()`: "You can't raise validation errors in Model.clean() for fields that don't appear in a model form ... Doing so will raise a ValueError ... To work around this dilemma, instead override Model.clean_fields() as it receives the list of fields that are excluded from validation."
  * Rendering of the admin login, index, user list, user change form and group pages in de-ch with Django's test client (Django 6.1.2, test database): no "Django", no "Normalisiert", no "ß", no hidden field, no "ID" column in the user list (tests in `leg_platform/accounts/tests/test_admin.py` and `test_translations.py`).
  * `AdminSite.site_header`, `site_title` and `index_title` default to "Django administration", "Django site admin" and "Site administration" (installed Django 6.1.2 source `django/contrib/admin/sites.py`).
  * `ModelAdmin.log_addition()`, `log_change()` and `log_deletions()` are the logging hooks of the admin in Django 6.0.9 and 6.1.2; the change view, the add view, the delete view and the delete action call them, and their default implementation stores `str(obj)[:200]` through `LogEntry.objects.log_actions()`. The admin index ("Recent actions") shows the stored `object_repr`; the history page shows the actor with `action.user.get_username` and, if present, `get_full_name` at display time; `accounts.User` has no `get_full_name()`, so only the email address (the login identifier) appears (installed sources `django/contrib/admin/options.py`, `actions.py`, `models.py`, templates `admin/index.html` and `admin/object_history.html`).
  * `ModelAdmin.get_form()` builds the form from the fields of `fieldsets`; fields that are not listed are not part of the form and are not changed on saving (installed Django 6.1.2 source `django/contrib/admin/options.py`; test of the hidden values).
  * `msgfmt` is not available locally; the compiled catalogue was produced with a script that reproduces the previously committed `.mo` byte for byte from its `.po` and checked with Python's `gettext.GNUTranslations`. CI checks it with GNU `msgfmt --check` (D-034).
* Security implications: the customer number is shown in the admin but never stored in admin log entries; these keep only the internal number (D-018, `docs/SECURITY.md`, section 10). Tests check the stored text of every logging hook and that hidden and read-only fields ignore posted values. The search by customer number runs on the queryset that already excludes staff and administrator accounts for staff users (test). The postal code form field is written only through the change form, whose permission checks are unchanged. The duplicate-address message must not be used by public registration (L-9).
* Alternatives: removing the unused eCH-0010 fields or merging the postal codes in the database (first version of this decision; rejected by the project owner: the eCH-0010 structure stays, unused fields are hidden); the internal ID as account display (replaced: the customer number is the number users and staff know); the customer number in admin log entries (rejected by the project owner after the security review of 2026-10-12, M-1: contradicts D-018).
* Sources: https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.1/ref/models/instances/, https://www.ech.ch/de/ech/ech-0010 (main document "STAN_d_DEF_2025-06-23_eCH-0010_V8.1.0_Datenstandard Postadresse.pdf" and `eCH-0010-8-0.xsd`), https://www.post.ch/de/geschaeftlich/themen-a-z/adressen-pflegen-und-geodaten-nutzen/adress-und-geodaten, https://www.swisstopo.admin.ch/de/amtliches-ortschaftenverzeichnis (all checked on 2026-10-12); D-032 (eCH-0010 schema `eCH-0010-8-0.xsd`, version 8.1.0); installed Django 6.1.2 sources named above; project owner decisions of 2026-10-12.
* Consequences: `docs/PROJECT.md` ("Profile Data and Purpose") and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` ("User Model and Login Identifiers") describe the shown and hidden fields. Registration and profile forms must show the same fields, the single postal code field and the generic duplicate handling (L-9).

## D-037: Registration, Verification Links, Login Views and Instance Links

* Date: 2026-10-13
* Status: Proposed (implemented for review; the points under "To be confirmed by the project owner" are not decided yet). Real email delivery could not be tested on the development machine; the flow is tested with Django's in-memory email backend only. Password reset, request limits and background sending followed in D-038.
* Context: README "Current Status", first open item: registration, email verification and the portal login and logout views (`docs/PROJECT.md`, "Registration"; D-016, D-021, D-025). Password reset (D-038), password change, the TOTP flow and Microsoft Graph delivery are separate items; rate limits and background sending were added afterwards by D-038, which supersedes the statements below about them.
* Decision:
  * Pages: `/` (leads to the login page, or to the profile when logged in), `/login/`, `/logout/` (POST only), `/register/`, `/register/sent/`, `/register/verify/<token>/`, `/register/complete/`, `/profile/` (read only, always `request.user`, no identifier in the URL). Pages with forms, tokens or personal data send `Cache-Control: no-store` (Django `never_cache`).
  * Registration: a form with an explicit field list (email address and the profile fields of `docs/PROJECT.md`, one postal code field, D-036), so that other submitted values are ignored. The response is always a redirect to the same generic page. A new or still unverified address creates an unverified account and a token and sends the link; a pending registration of the same address is replaced (the old link stops working). An address of an active, temporarily locked or blocked account creates no account and receives an information email without a link; deactivated accounts do not hold their address (D-016). A concurrent registration that loses the unique index is retried and then finds the winner. Duplicate handling never produces a form error (L-9).
  * Verification token: 256 bits from `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)` (43 characters), stored only as an HMAC-SHA256 digest with a key derived from `SECRET_KEY` via HKDF with its own label (D-023), in the table `VerificationToken` (purpose, digest, expiry, user). Opening the link only stores the digest in the session and redirects to `/register/complete/`, so that the token is not in the URL of the page that is shown (docs/SECURITY.md, section 6; the approach of Django's password reset view) and mail scanners cannot consume it. The token is consumed, and the account activated, only when the form with the password is submitted successfully; two concurrent submissions are serialised with a row lock and only one succeeds. The form shows the verified address read-only and lets the person correct the profile and choose a password (Django password validators, minimum length 15, `docs/SECURITY.md`, section 3). No automatic login: the person logs in through the login page, which will later include the second factor.
  * `User.complete_registration()` writes password, status and customer number together through the central account logic: an account is never active without a usable password, and the customer number is issued only here (D-021).
  * Expiry: unverified accounts and their tokens are deleted by the management command `delete_expired_registrations` (not scheduled yet, like `cleanup_login_ip_attempts`); the status is checked again under a lock, so an active account is never deleted.
  * Token redaction: a logging filter on the handler of all loggers replaces the token in `/register/verify/<token>/` in log messages, arguments and exception texts (Django logs the path of every response with status 400 or higher, and the development server logs every request line). The README deployment gate for token redaction is fulfilled for this URL by the filter and its tests; every later token URL must be added to the pattern. Reverse proxies and web servers keep their own access logs, which the operator configures (to be documented in the installation guide).
  * Email: plain text, written in the default language of the instance, sent through Django's email framework (`leg_platform/accounts/emails.py`). Links are built from `INSTANCE_BASE_URL`, never from the request. A failing send is logged by exception class only (no address, link or message), and the response stays generic; the person can register again.
  * Instance configuration (environment, D-013, `.env.example`): `INSTANCE_NAME` (default "LEG Platform", at most 100 characters, no control or line separator characters because it appears in email subjects), `INSTANCE_BASE_URL` (an `https://` origin without path or credentials; `http://` additionally with `APP_ENV=local`, default `http://127.0.0.1:8000` locally; required elsewhere; its host must be in `DJANGO_ALLOWED_HOSTS`), `INSTANCE_PRIVACY_URL` (absolute `https://` URL, required outside `APP_ENV=local`) and `INSTANCE_WEBSITE_URL` (optional). Invalid values prevent the start (`docs/DESIGN.md`, section 3.2). The privacy notice is linked on every page without login, including the lockout page.
  * Login: Django's `LoginView` with a form that shows one message for every failure (wrong password, unknown, unverified, blocked or deactivated account): `EmailModelBackend` returns no user for inactive accounts, so they are indistinguishable. `redirect_authenticated_user` stays off (docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, "Deliberately Open"). Redirect targets are checked by Django against the request host. The lockout page of Axes is a template without the submitted value (`AXES_LOCKOUT_TEMPLATE`). Logout accepts POST only (Django `LogoutView`).
  * Interface: Tabler 1.6.1 (`dist/css/tabler.min.css`, MIT) is served from `leg_platform/static/vendor/tabler/` with its licence, together with Source Sans 3, Bricolage Grotesque and IBM Plex Mono (unmodified TTF files and OFL texts from the official repository `google/fonts`) and the Tabler Icons SVG used so far (`lock`, v3.49.0, MIT, inlined as a template). A small own stylesheet sets the platform defaults of `docs/DESIGN.md` (accent, 16 px text, 48 px controls, status dots, the two registers). The login page follows the project owner's mockup of 2026-10-11 ("LEG-Platform Designsprache", version 3): a tinted "Warm" welcome area on the left, the "Clear" form card on the right with the primary button, the "No account yet?" divider and the outlined registration button, and the links below the card; the "Password forgotten?" link, the imprint link and the instance logo are not shown yet (reset and imprint do not exist, D-022; the logo is instance data, `docs/DESIGN.md`, section 3.3). No JavaScript, no inline script or style (a test checks every page). Tabler's stylesheet contains 23 `data:` images (select arrow, checkbox and similar), which the policy `img-src 'self'` (D-028) blocks; the select arrow is replaced by an own static SVG, and no other control that needs one is used yet.
* Verified facts:
  * The tarball of `@tabler/core` 1.6.1 from the official npm registry has the SHA-512 integrity value published in the registry metadata (compared on 2026-10-13); its `package.json` states MIT; the repository `LICENSE` (https://github.com/tabler/tabler) is copied next to the CSS. The package contains no top-level licence file.
  * Installed Django 6.1.2: `SetPasswordMixin`, `AuthenticationForm`, `LoginView` and `LogoutView` (`django/contrib/auth/forms.py`, `views.py`) behave as used here; `LogoutView` accepts POST only; `django.server` logs request lines through the logger `django.server` and `django.request` logs the path of responses with status 400 or higher (`django/utils/log.py`). The installed Axes 8.3.1 renders `AXES_LOCKOUT_TEMPLATE` with the status `AXES_HTTP_RESPONSE_CODE` and passes the submitted username in its context, which the template does not show.
  * Tests: `leg_platform/accounts/tests/test_registration.py`, `test_portal_views.py`, `leg_platform/tests/test_instance_configuration.py` (registration, replacement, generic responses, privileges ignored, token storage, expiry, single use, concurrency, password rules, login failures, redirects, lockout, logout, profile isolation, caching, CSP, Swiss German without "ß", token redaction, configuration validation).
* Security implications and open points (also in the README, "Current Status"):
  * The login does not check a second factor yet. Staff users and system administrators can log in with the password alone, and the same session opens the admin, which stays restricted to `APP_ENV=local` (`LocalAdminOnlyMiddleware`; removed by D-052). The TOTP flow must call Django `login()` only after all required factors (D-017).
  * Mail clients or scanners that open the link do not activate anything; a person who forwards the link still needs the form and a password of their own choice.
  * Not implemented: Swiss address validation (D-033), dark mode and the colour mode switch, instance branding (logo, accent and highlight colour from the configuration; where these values are kept is open, `docs/DESIGN.md`, section 11), the member navigation (menu and tab bar), the print stylesheet beyond hiding the navigation, a visible supported-browsers list, and Microsoft Graph delivery (D-006). The welcome text on the login page announces locations, energy data and invoices "later"; it is taken from the mockup and describes later phases.
* Security review of 2026-10-13 (agent `security-reviewer`, read only, started by the main session; no critical finding) and how it was handled:
  * H-1 (fixed): the emails went to the entered spelling while the account is keyed by the canonical form. Django encodes domains with the IDNA 2003 codec, the account key uses UTS #46 (checked: `faß.example` becomes `fass.example` for the mail and `xn--fa-hia.example` for the key), so an attacker could bind an account to an address on a domain they do not control the mailbox of. Both registration emails now go to the canonical stored address; tests cover upper case, white space and `ß`. Consequence for D-035: the mail also goes to the lower-cased local part, which closes the takeover through a mailbox `Max@x` (a person who controls only `Max@x` on a host that distinguishes case cannot verify the address `max@x`); a person whose provider distinguishes case in local parts and who typed upper case will not receive the mail. The owner decision of D-035 (lower-casing the local part) was re-confirmed on 2026-10-13.
  * L-1 (fixed): the completion form is bound to the registration it was rendered for (hidden reference compared with the token of the session); a later link opened in the same browser can no longer receive the data of a form rendered for an earlier one. Sessions are database-backed, so the digest in the session stays on the server.
  * L-2 (fixed): `sensitive_post_parameters` for the password fields. L-3 (fixed): the invalid-link page is marked as not cacheable. Deviation from `docs/SECURITY.md`, section 6: for an invalid or expired link the page is rendered at the token URL (as Django's password reset view does); it contains no token and no personal data. The redirect away from the token happens for valid links.
  * L-4 (fixed): an address of an account that is not pending is read without a lock; a pending registration is locked and re-checked; the completion locks the account before the token (the same order as the replacement); deadlocks and lost races are retried like unique-index conflicts. Two real concurrent submissions and a concurrent replacement are tested.
  * L-5 (fixed): the cleanup also deletes unverified accounts that have no valid link, continues after a failing account, logs only the exception class and exits non-zero when an account failed. Until it is scheduled, "deleted automatically" in `docs/SECURITY.md`, section 3, is not yet true (README, deployment gate 5).
  * L-6 (fixed): the response comparison now covers status, location, content, cookies and headers of the POST responses; the temporarily locked case, the real request-log redaction, the no-store header of the invalid pages and the token binding are tested.
  * L-7 (handled): rotating `SECRET_KEY` invalidates every pending link (the keyed digest has no fallback keys; a person registers again; accepted, like the reset of the login counters in D-023). The vendored files have a checksum list (`leg_platform/static/vendor/CHECKSUMS.txt`). `|safe` was removed from the form template; only the password rules list, which Django builds with escaping, is marked safe, in the form.
  * M-1 (six digits confirmed by the project owner on 2026-10-13; monitoring still open): customer numbers are issued at the completion of a registration, and nothing limits how many registrations one person can complete with a catch-all mail domain; the rate limits of D-038 now bound the requests per address and per client address. The statement in D-021 that capacity is "not limited in practice" holds only without deliberate abuse; at exhaustion the completion fails with an error (500). Warning level added on 2026-10-13 (D-038); not implemented: a defined error page at exhaustion.
  * Known gaps the reviewer listed as open requirements, not new findings: TOTP, email change, session timeouts (`SESSION_COOKIE_AGE` is Django's default of two weeks), the security log, Microsoft Graph delivery and the scheduling of the cleanup commands.
* Review of the duplicate registration (agent `security-reviewer`, 2026-10-13, question of the project owner; no critical finding): an address that already has an account gets the same response as any other address and an information email; the person typing learns nothing. The information email is one text for active, temporarily locked and blocked accounts, so that nobody who reads the mailbox learns the state: it says that an account exists, links the login page (from `INSTANCE_BASE_URL`, no token) and points to the password reset; it contains no IP address, user agent or time of the requester and asks for no reply, because third parties can trigger it. The confirmation page does not claim that a link was sent, and both emails carry `Auto-Submitted: auto-generated` (RFC 3834, section 5.2) on the SMTP path; the Microsoft Graph backend does not send this header (D-006, decided 2026-10-14). Tests compare status, content, cookies and header values for new, active, blocked, temporarily locked, deactivated and pending addresses and a failing send. The limits, the identical request path with a background worker and the sender address were implemented in D-038.
* To be confirmed by the project owner:
  * Lifetime of the verification link: 24 hours (proposal; `docs/PROJECT.md` and `docs/SECURITY.md` state no value).
  * Required `INSTANCE_PRIVACY_URL` outside `APP_ENV=local` (proposal, so that no instance goes live without the notice required by D-022).
  * Decided by the project owner on 2026-10-13: `data:` images are not allowed in `img-src` (best practice; the policy stays `'self'`, D-028). OWASP's CSP cheat sheet gives `img-src 'self'` in its tightened basic policy and warns that a permissive policy is likely to lead to bypasses (checked 2026-10-13). Images that a library embeds as `data:` are replaced by static files.
* Sources: installed sources of Django 6.1.2 and django-axes 8.3.1 named above; https://registry.npmjs.org/@tabler/core/1.6.1 and https://github.com/tabler/tabler (`LICENSE`), checked on 2026-10-13; https://github.com/google/fonts (`ofl/sourcesans3`, `ofl/bricolagegrotesque`, `ofl/ibmplexmono`) and https://github.com/tabler/tabler-icons (tag v3.49.0, `icons/outline/lock.svg`, `LICENSE`), downloaded on 2026-10-13; the mockup provided by the project owner on 2026-10-13; https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authentication_Cheat_Sheet.html (generic responses, cited in D-016); D-016, D-017, D-021, D-023, D-024, D-025, D-026, D-028.

## D-038: Password Reset, Request Limits, Background Sending and Sender Address

* Date: 2026-10-13
* Status: Proposed (implemented for review; the values under "To be confirmed by the project owner" are proposals). Real email delivery could not be tested on the development machine; everything is tested with Django's in-memory email backend only.
* Context: open points of D-037 and of the review of the duplicate registration: per-address and per-IP limits (D-017), response time independent of the state of an address (`docs/SECURITY.md`, section 3), password reset, the sender address. Decisions of the project owner of 2026-10-13: limits as best practice for this kind of application, sending in the background, the sender address is configured by the administrator (otherwise `noreply@<domain>`), the reset flow is implemented.
* Decision:
  * Outbox: a request for registration or password reset counts the request and stores one `MailJob` (kind, normalised address, attempts, next due time). It reads no account and sends nothing, so the response and its time do not depend on the address. The response is the same redirect and generic page as before. The worker (`manage.py process_mail_queue`, `--loop` for a service) takes a job under a lock (attempt counter raised and next due time moved forward; this is the lease and the retry delay of 2 minutes times the attempts), lets the handler look up the address and store what it needs in one transaction, sends outside any transaction and deletes the job. Raw tokens are created by the worker and sent at once; they are never stored in a job. Delivery is at least once; after 3 attempts a job is given up, and unprocessed jobs of requests are deleted after 1 hour and unprocessed notifications after 24 hours by `cleanup_mail_requests`. A database error while claiming ends the round without raising.
  * Limits (proposed values; OWASP's Forgot Password Cheat Sheet names the controls, not the numbers): per normalised address 12 requests per hour and 24 per 24 hours (project owner, 2026-10-13: the first hour must allow a person at the device several attempts; first version: 3 and 10) **and, since the review of phase 3 (A2), at most 3 per 10 minutes** (a burst window against a script that uses up the hour at once), shared by registration and password reset (and email change); per client IP address 20 per hour (IPv6 counted by its /64 prefix, RFC 6177; no determinable address: one shared bucket, a warning is logged); instance wide 300 per hour for registration and 300 per hour for password reset, counted separately (reaching it is logged). A request counts only if every limit has room, in one transaction with ordered row locks, so that a refused client does not use up the budget of someone else's address and parallel requests cannot exceed a limit. **Since the review of phase 3 (A1) the limits of the client address and of the instance are checked first, read only, and a request over one of them is refused before anything is stored (no job, no bucket; the refusal is logged at most once a minute); only these two are shown to the person (HTTP 429); the limit of an address is not shown (D-058).** A request over the limit of an address is stored as a job without the address and processed as "send nothing", so it changes nothing visible and replaces no pending registration. The information email to an existing address is sent at most once per address per 24 hours, checked in the worker. Counters are rows in `RateLimitBucket` and `RateLimitEvent` under a row lock per key, with keyed digests (HKDF from `SECRET_KEY`, D-023) instead of addresses or IP addresses; unused buckets are deleted after 24 hours. Deviation from D-026: a database table instead of Django's cache framework, because cache counters are not atomic and the same pattern is already used for the login IP counter (D-017).
  * Password reset: pages `/password-reset/`, `/password-reset/sent/`, `/password-reset/verify/<token>/` and `/password-reset/new-password/` follow the registration pattern (token only as keyed digest, the link stores the digest in the session and redirects, the token is consumed only when the form with the new password is submitted successfully, `no-store`, `no-referrer`, token redaction in logs). The email is sent only to an account with the status active (a temporarily locked account has this status, so reset works during a lockout; unknown, unverified, blocked and deactivated addresses receive nothing, and the person typing cannot tell). The lookup uses the normalised unique column. The link is valid for 24 hours, equal to the longest limit window, so that a person who cannot request another email because someone else used up the budget can still use a link already in the mailbox; several links can be outstanding, and a completed reset removes all of them. A reset ends all other sessions (Django derives the session hash from the password) and the temporary lockout of the account only, never the counters and lockouts of IP addresses (D-017); the second factor is untouched. The account receives a notification of the changed password.
  * Information email for existing addresses now says that an account exists, links the login page and tells the person that a new password can be requested on the login page if the password is unknown (identical text for active, temporarily locked and blocked accounts).
  * Sender: `EMAIL_SENDER_ADDRESS` (environment, optional) checked against a strict address pattern without control characters; default `noreply@<host of INSTANCE_BASE_URL>`, locally `noreply@localhost`. The emails never state that replies are read (project owner, 2026-10-13: automatic emails expect no reply); the notification of a changed password names the contact information of the instance (`INSTANCE_WEBSITE_URL`) where configured. SPF, DKIM and DMARC are DNS settings of the sending domain and are documented in the installation guide, not enforced by the code.
  * Management commands: `process_mail_queue`, `cleanup_mail_requests` and `delete_expired_registrations`; their scheduling is open (deployment gate).
  * Customer number warning (project owner, 2026-10-13): from 90 % of the six-digit numbers issued (`CUSTOMER_NUMBER_WARNING_PERCENT`), every issue of a number logs a warning ("N of 1000000 customer numbers have been issued (P%)", no personal data), and the user list of the administration shows a notice to system administrators (not to staff users). The count is the one `issue()` already makes. The notice is visible only where the administration is reachable (since D-052 in every environment, earlier `APP_ENV=local` only, D-004); until then the log line is the alarm and the operator must watch for it. Reaching 100 % still fails the completion of a registration with an error (D-037, M-1).
* Verified facts:
  * Django 6.1 refuses `EMAIL_TIMEOUT` together with `MAILERS`, so the setting was removed; a Graph backend has to set its own timeout. Django 6.1's `check` command accesses the database.
  * All tests pass with Django 6.1.2 and with the minimum Django 6.0.9, MariaDB 12.3, Python 3.14, deprecation warnings as errors: 455 tests (2026-10-13). `manage.py check --deploy` with a production-like environment reports no issue; `makemigrations --check` finds no changes; the Swiss German catalogue has no "ß" and passes `msgfmt --check`.
  * Tests cover: identical responses for every state of the address, an identical set of database statements for every state (compared as a set, because the buckets are locked in digest order), no database transaction open while sending, leases and retries, crash and database error behaviour, parallel requests against a limit, IPv6 prefixes, the suppressed jobs, sender validation, reset single use, session end, lockout handling and the unchanged IP counters.
* Security review (agent `security-reviewer`, started by the main session): its findings on the first version led to: ordered bucket locks and a single transaction for the three limits, jobs of refused requests without an address, the lease and the sending outside any transaction, catching database errors in the worker, and the lock order account before token. The corrected version was not reviewed again yet.
* Security implications and open points:
  * Plain-text addresses are held in unprocessed jobs for at most one hour. A database dump taken in that time contains them.
  * Delivery is at least once; an email can arrive twice after a crash. Tokens are single use, so a duplicate is harmless.
  * If the worker does not run, no email is sent and nobody learns why; monitoring of the queue age is not implemented. Requests within the limits are counted even when the worker is down.
  * An attacker who knows an address can use up its budget (12 per hour, 24 per day) and so keep the owner from requesting a new email for a while; the 24-hour link lifetime and the per-address limit of the information email limit the effect (D-017, accepted).
  * The IP limit depends on the correct number of trusted proxies (`AXES_IPWARE_PROXY_COUNT`); an office behind one address shares the budget.
  * The request rate of the pages themselves is not limited by the application; the reverse proxy must do it.
* Decided by the project owner on 2026-10-13: the hourly per-address limit of 12 (the daily value of 24 and the other limits are my proposals and still to be confirmed); no explicit sender is required: without `EMAIL_SENDER_ADDRESS` the default `noreply@<host>` applies in every environment; no separate instance contact value (the information email has no contact line; the sender address is the only instance address, and it is not announced as a reply address unless set explicitly); the customer number stays six digits (D-021, D-037 M-1); the lower-casing of the local part stays (D-035); `data:` images stay disallowed (D-037, D-028). Still to be confirmed: the 24-hour lifetime of the reset link and the daily and IP limits.
* Sources: OWASP Forgot Password Cheat Sheet and Authentication Cheat Sheet (https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/); RFC 6177 (IPv6 end-site assignments), RFC 3834 (Auto-Submitted); installed Django 6.1.2 and 6.0.9 sources and release notes (https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.1/releases/6.1/); D-016, D-017, D-023, D-026, D-037; project owner decisions of 2026-10-13.

## D-039: Editable Email Texts in the Administration

* Date: 2026-10-13
* Status: Decided by the project owner (the answers below, 2026-10-13); implemented; security review of 2026-10-13 below.
* Context: the project owner wants to change or rewrite the texts of the emails that the application sends (registration link, information for an existing address, password reset link, password change notification) in the administration, with a list of the placeholders that can be used and a check of the placeholders when saving.
* Decision (project owner, 2026-10-13):
  * Only the default language of the instance (Swiss German) is editable; one text per email. Subject and body are editable.
  * The editor refuses to save a text without the personal link of the email (`{verification_url}` for the registration, `{reset_url}` for the password reset).
  * System administrators and staff users with the permission "change email text" may edit (permission `accounts.change_emailtext`, assigned to a role at runtime as for every permission, D-012; nothing is hard-coded). The history of an email text is visible to system administrators only, as for accounts.
  * Every text can be reset to the default (a checkbox, or an empty text), and every change is recorded in the administration log with the name of the email and the changed field names, never with the text.
  * Web and email addresses cannot be typed into a text or subject: after NFKC normalisation and removal of format characters, the editor rejects schemes (`https:`, `mailto:`, `tel:` and others), `//`, backslashes, `@` and any word with a dot followed by two letters (so also `www.`, bare domains and email addresses). It is a deny-list and cannot be complete (look-alike letters of other scripts pass); the permission to edit is the protection against a person who wants to bypass it. Addresses reach a text only through placeholders.
* Implementation:
  * Table `EmailText` (one row per email, created empty on first view of the list; an empty subject or body means the default). The defaults stay in the application (the four templates and the subjects); a text equal to the default is stored as empty, so that later improvements of the default reach the instance.
  * Placeholders are written `{name}`. All emails: `{instance_name}`, `{contact_url}` (the instance's website, empty if not configured), `{privacy_url}`, `{login_url}`; the registration and password reset emails also `{lifetime_hours}` and their own link. The subject can use `{instance_name}` only. The list and the editor show them (the list for all emails, the editor for the email being edited).
  * The saved text is never run through the template engine: the markers are replaced in a single pass from a fixed list of values by a regular expression, so a text cannot read variables, call anything or expand a value again. A text with `{{ ... }}`, `{% ... %}` or any unknown or unbalanced bracket is refused (the name of the offending placeholder is shown).
  * Protection at sending time: if a saved text no longer passes the validation (for example after a later change of the placeholders), the default is used and a warning with the name of the email (not the text) is logged, so that a missing link never reaches a person.
  * Maximum 5000 characters for the text, 150 for the subject; no control, format or separator characters (Unicode categories C* and Zl/Zp; the text may contain line breaks and tabs).
* Verified facts: the full test suite (510 tests) passes with Django 6.1.2 on MariaDB 12.3; it was not run with Django 6.0.9 after this change. The `ModelAdmin` hooks used (`changelist_view`, `has_*_permission`, `history_view`, `change_list_template`) exist in both versions. The administration stays restricted to `APP_ENV=local` (D-004; superseded by D-052).
* Security implications and risks:
  * Anyone who may change the texts decides what the emails say, including emails that carry real links. They cannot add other addresses (rule above), but they can write misleading text. The permission is therefore not part of any default role; give it only to people who are trusted with the communication of the instance, and review it in the access review.
  * The system-administrator-only history and the log entries without text keep the audit trail free of personal data (D-018).
  * Not implemented: language variants and a diff between versions.
* Further decisions of the project owner on 2026-10-13: the password change notification gets no special treatment (the same permission as every other text); a preview and a test email are wanted; a version history is wanted; no approval by a second person (too few administrators; the instance will often be run by one person).
* Preview and test email: the button "Preview" in the editor validates the typed text like a save and shows the subject and text as they would be sent, with example links (the token in them does not exist), without saving, logging or sending anything. From the preview, "Send a test email" sends the typed text to the address of the logged-in account only (never to a typed address), with the prefix "[Test]" and a note that the personal link does not work; a failing send is reported without details and logged by exception class only.
